# The Selmer converse for elliptic curves at every prime

Coordinate `EN-101-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M1-C-AL-M-0004` | `PM-002.2` | status ACTIVE | original slug `The-Selmer-converse-for-elliptic-curves-at-every-prime-October-7-2026` | date 2026-10-07 | derived from the openai/math preprint The-Selmer-converse-for-elliptic-curves-at-every-prime-October-7-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove the Selmer converse in coranks zero and one for every elliptic curve over ℚ and every prime <i>p</i>: if the full <i>p</i>-power Selmer group has ℤ<sub><i>p</i></sub>-corank $`r\\in\\{0,1\\}`$, then the analytic and Mordell–Weil ranks both equal <i>r</i>, and the entire Tate–Shafarevich group is finite. As an application at the additive prime 3, we prove that for every prime $`\\ell\\equiv4,7,8\\pmod9`$, the cubic $`X^3+Y^3=\\ell Z^3`$ has analytic and Mordell–Weil rank one and finite Tate–Shafarevich group. In particular, every such <i>ℓ</i> is a sum of two rational cubes.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Selmer converse in coranks zero and one for every elliptic curve over ℚ and every prime <i>p</i>: if the full <i>p</i>-power Selmer group has ℤ<sub><i>p</i></sub>-corank $`r\in\{0,1\}`$, then the analytic and Mordell–Weil ranks both equal <i>r</i>, and the entire Tate–Shafarevich group is finite. As an application at the additive prime 3, we prove that for every prime $`\ell\equiv4,7,8\pmod9`$, the cubic $`X^3+Y^3=\ell Z^3`$ has analytic and Mordell–Weil rank one and finite Tate–Shafarevich group. In particular, every such <i>ℓ</i> is a sum of two rational cubes.

- **claim-unrestricted-selmer-converse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every elliptic curve $A_0/\mathbb Q$, every prime $p$, and $r\in\{0,1\}$, $$s_p(A_0)=r \quad\Longrightarrow\quad a(A_0)=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_{\mathbb Z}A_0(\mathbb Q)=r, \qquad \#\operatorname{Sha}(A_0/\mathbb Q)<\infty.$$ There is no restriction on reduction type, complex multiplication, the residual representation, rational torsion, or rational isogenies.
  - Explanation: Full prime-power Selmer corank zero or one determines both ranks and implies finiteness of the entire Tate–Shafarevich group for elliptic curves over the rationals.
- **claim-established-even-prime-converse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-two-converse-input)): If $E/\mathbb Q$ is any elliptic curve and $\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb Z_2}\mathop{\mathrm{Sel}}_{2^\infty}(E/\mathbb Q)=r\in\{0,1\}$, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(E,s)=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}E(\mathbb Q)=r, \qquad \operatorname{Sha}(E/\mathbb Q)\text{ is finite}.$$
  - Explanation: The established converse at the even prime supplies rank equality and finiteness under the stated full Selmer corank hypothesis.
- **claim-analytic-twist-densities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-twist-density-input)): For any elliptic curve $E/\mathbb Q$, among nonzero squarefree integers $d$ ordered by $\|d\|$, the quadratic twists $E^{(d)}$ of analytic rank zero and one each have density $1/2$. In particular the exceptional set of twists of analytic rank at least two has density zero.
  - Explanation: The established density theorem provides equally frequent analytic rank-zero and rank-one twists and a density-zero higher-rank exceptional set.
- **claim-compatible-auxiliary-fields** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-auxiliary-fields)): There are coprime negative odd fundamental discriminants $D,D'$ such that every prime dividing $2Np$ splits in both quadratic fields, $$\left(\frac{D}{\|D'\|}\right)=1,$$ and each of $A_0^D,A_0^{D'},A_0^{DD'}$ has the smallest analytic rank permitted by its sign. They may also be chosen so that $$K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt D),\qquad F=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{DD'}),\qquad L=\mathbb Q(\sqrt D,\sqrt{D'})$$ satisfy the following properties: $L/F$ is CM and unramified at every finite prime, $L$ has only the roots of unity $\{\pm1\}$, and $L$ does not contain the CM field of $A_0$ if $A_0$ has CM.
  - Explanation: Auxiliary discriminants simultaneously satisfy splitting, minimal twist-rank, unramified CM-extension, and field-exclusion requirements.
- **claim-heegner-arithmetic-reduction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-arithmetic-reduction)): With these choices, $$\dim H^1_f(K,V)=1, \qquad \dim H^1_f(K,V^{DD'})=1.$$ The second line localizes nontrivially at both places of $K$ over $p$. Let $y\in A_0(K)$ be the norm of the conductor-one Heegner point under a modular parametrization taking the cusp $\infty$ to zero. If $y$ is non-torsion, Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> follows.
  - Explanation: The auxiliary choices give the required compact Selmer lines, and a non-torsion conductor-one Heegner trace suffices for the main conclusion.
- **claim-base-greenberg-dichotomy** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-base-green)): If the compact Selmer line of $W/K$ has nonzero localization, then $H^1_{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}(K,W)=H^1_{\overline{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}}(K,W)=0$. The only remaining possibility is the following *strict case* in the $V$ summand: $r=1$, and the compact Selmer space is generated by a class $a$ such that $a^c=a$ and every localization of $a$ is zero. In that case $$\label{eq:base-green-bases} H^1_{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}(K,V)=\langle a,h\rangle, \qquad H^1_{\overline{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}}(K,V)=\langle a,h^c\rangle,$$ where $h$ has nonzero localization at $v$ and zero localization at $v^c$. The $V^{DD'}$ summand always has zero Greenberg space.
  - Explanation: Nonzero localization forces both one-sided Greenberg groups to vanish; the remaining strict case has the specified base spaces and occurs only in the original summand.
- **claim-controlled-tame-primes** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-tame-primes)): There are rational primes $q_i\notin S$, split completely in $L$, distinguished places $\mathfrak q_i$ of $K$ above them, cyclic $p$-quotients $\Delta_i$ of the ring class group of conductor $q_i$, and surjections $$\ell_i:G_K\longrightarrow\Delta_i\simeq\mathbb Z/p^{n_i}\mathbb Z, \qquad n_i\longrightarrow\infty,$$ with the following properties.* 1. *$\ell_i$ is unramified outside the pair above $q_i$, its restriction to inertia at $\mathfrak q_i$ is surjective, $\ell_i^c=-\ell_i$, and $\ell_i\|_{G_{K_s}}=0$ for every $s\in S$.* 2. *$v_p(q_i-1)-n_i$ is bounded. At either place above $p$, the full ring class field of conductor $q_i$ is unramified and its local degree has bounded $p$-part.* 3. *Frobenius at $\mathfrak q_i$, on all fixed data unramified outside $S$, tends to an element $\gamma\in G_L$ such that $$\epsilon(\gamma)=1,\qquad \det(V(\gamma)-1)\ne0,\qquad \mathcal C(\gamma)\text{ is not a root of unity}.$$ Moreover $\gamma$ fixes all $p$-power radicals of a fixed, conjugation-stable finite list in $K^\times$ spanning $\mathcal O_{K,S}^{\times}\otimes\mathbb Q_p$ under the Kummer map.
  - Explanation: The auxiliary prime sequence supplies growing cyclic tame quotients, triviality at fixed places, bounded local degree losses, and a controlled limiting Frobenius element.
- **claim-admissible-specialization-and-division** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-admissible-specialization)): For constant coefficients $V$, admissible $H^1$ is precisely $H^1(G_{K,S},V)$, and likewise over $L$. For $\mathbb Q_p(1)$ the same assertion holds when unramifiedness at the moving primes is imposed. The fixed local conditions specialize to the usual rational local conditions. Coefficient reduction and multiplication by $t$ give short exact sequences of admissible cochain complexes $$\label{eq:coefficient-sequence} 0\longrightarrow C^\bullet(M_{b-1}^{\eta}) \xrightarrow{\ t\ } C^\bullet(M_b^{\eta}) \longrightarrow C^\bullet(V\otimes k)\longrightarrow0.$$ Zero local conditions at $p$ and local Kummer conditions are preserved by the resulting division of a class with zero specialization by $t$.
  - Explanation: Constant-coefficient admissible cohomology specializes to ordinary continuous cohomology, while coefficient division preserves the fixed strict and Kummer conditions.
- **claim-necessary-obstruction-vanishing** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-obstruction-necessary)): The expression $H_\gamma$ is independent of the primitive and bilinear. If a nonzero class $x\in H^1_{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}(K,V\otimes k)$ is the specialization of an admissible class in $H^1_{\mathrm{Gr}}(K,M_2^{\eta})$, then $$\label{eq:obstruction-vanishing} H_\gamma(x,d)=0 \quad\text{for every }d\in H^1_{\overline{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}}(K,V\otimes k).$$ The same necessary condition holds for either $\eta$.
  - Explanation: The corrected pairing is well defined and bilinear, and an admissible first-order lift must annihilate every class with the opposite Greenberg condition.
- **claim-nondegenerate-tame-obstruction** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-nonzero-obstruction)): The element $\gamma$ in <a href="#prop:tame-primes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:tame-primes">7</a> can be chosen so that, in the bases of <a href="#eq:base-green-bases" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:base-green-bases">[eq:base-green-bases]</a>, the obstruction matrix is $$\label{eq:obstruction-matrix} \begin{pmatrix}0&\kappa\\-\kappa&0\end{pmatrix}, \qquad \kappa\in\mathbb Q_p^\times.$$ Thus no nonzero base Greenberg class lifts to order two.
  - Explanation: A commutator modification makes the obstruction pairing nondegenerate, preventing any nonzero base Greenberg class from lifting to order two.
- **claim-uniform-greenberg-length** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-green-length)): For the primes of <a href="#prop:tame-primes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:tame-primes">7</a>, chosen in the strict case as in <a href="#lem:nonzero-obstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:nonzero-obstruction">11</a>, every fixed $b\ge1$, and either $\eta=1$ or $\eta=-1$, one has $$\label{eq:green-length} \mathop{\mathrm{length}}_{R_b}H^1_{\mathrm{Gr}}(L,V\otimes\psi^{\eta}) \begin{cases} =0,&\text{outside the strict case},\\ \le2,&\text{in the strict case}. \end{cases}$$ In the strict case this module is killed by $t$ and is the image of its two-dimensional base space under multiplication by $t^{b-1}$. Put $$\label{eq:b-star} b_*=1\quad\text{outside the strict case},\qquad b_*=3\quad\text{in the strict case}.$$ Thus in both cases the length in <a href="#eq:green-length" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:green-length">[eq:green-length]</a> is strictly smaller than $b_*$.
  - Explanation: The admissible Greenberg module vanishes outside the strict case and has uniformly bounded length in the strict case, giving an upper bound smaller than the chosen truncation.
- **claim-uniform-unramified-logarithms** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-uniform-local-log)): Fix an integer $d_0\ge0$. There is a constant $c$ such that, for every finite unramified extension $E/\mathbb Q_p$ with $v_p([E:\mathbb Q_p])\le d_0$, $$\label{eq:uniform-local-log} \log_{A_0,p}(A_0(E))\subseteq p^{-c}\mathcal O_E.$$ If $P\in A_0(E)$ and the Kummer class of $p^C P$ vanishes in $H^1(E,A_0[p^n])$, where $C,n\ge0$ are integers, then $$v_p(\log_{A_0,p}P)\ge n-C-c.$$
  - Explanation: A bound on the prime-power part of the unramified local degree gives uniform logarithm denominators and converts Kummer divisibility into logarithm precision.
- **claim-weighted-heegner-logarithm-vanishing** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-heegner-log)): At the distinguished place over $p$, and for either sign, $$\label{eq:heegner-log} \sum_{g\in\mathcal G_i}(1+t)^{\pm\ell_i(g)} \log_{A_0,p}(y_i^g) \longrightarrow0\pmod {t^{b_*}}.$$ More precisely, every coefficient of degree less than $b_*$ has valuation tending to $+\infty$ along $\mathcal U$. The coefficients through any other fixed degree have a common lower valuation bound. These assertions hold in a common $p$-adic algebraic closure, with its fixed extension of the valuation.
  - Explanation: Raw weighted Heegner logarithms vanish through the required tame order, while every fixed higher truncation retains a uniform lower valuation bound.
- **claim-split-parabolic-eigenvalues** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-split-u-operators)): Every test <a href="#eq:p-pivot-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:p-pivot-test">[eq:p-pivot-test]</a> satisfies $U_2\Phi_p=p^2\Phi_p$ and $U_3\Phi_p=p^2\Phi_p$. Thus both operators $p^{-2}U_j$ have value $1$ on the theta form of weight <a href="#eq:theta-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-weight">[eq:theta-weight]</a>.
  - Explanation: The pivot-support calculation gives the same exact eigenvalue for both parabolic operators and hence unit normalized eigenvalues independently of elliptic reduction.
- **claim-theta-spherical-parameter** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-theta-eigenparameters)): At a split spherical place the standard parameter of the theta form, in unitary normalization, is $$\label{eq:theta-eigenparameters} (\pi\otimes\eta)\ \oplus\ \|\cdot\|^{-1/2} \ \oplus\ \|\cdot\|^{1/2}.$$ Here $\pi\otimes\eta$ means tensoring the standard two-dimensional parameter by the determinant-twist character.
  - Explanation: At split spherical places, the theta parameter consists of the determinant-twisted elliptic block and the final pair of norm characters.
- **claim-integral-holomorphic-theta-family** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-theta-family)): For the characters <a href="#eq:theta-character" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-character">[eq:theta-character]</a> and every sufficiently large positive $m$ on the prescribed branch, the theta construction gives holomorphic algebraic forms $\Theta_{i,m}$ of weight <a href="#eq:theta-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-weight">[eq:theta-weight]</a>. In the canonical ordinary monomial-dual lattice their coefficients have a common bounded denominator, coefficientwise in the tame group algebra and in every fixed truncated substitution $R_b$. They have spherical parameters <a href="#eq:theta-eigenparameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-eigenparameters">[eq:theta-eigenparameters]</a>, and at both distinguished $p$-places they satisfy $$p^{-2}U_2\Theta_{i,m}=\Theta_{i,m},\qquad p^{-2}U_3\Theta_{i,m}=\Theta_{i,m}.$$ These assertions allow arbitrary fixed split tests of the stipulated bounded levels. They remain valid for the particular tests with all the finite local controls in Proposition <a href="#prop:bounded-content" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:bounded-content">27</a>.
  - Explanation: The theta construction produces holomorphic algebraic forms with uniform monomial-dual denominator control, specified spherical parameters, and normalized unit parabolic eigenvalues.
- **claim-bounded-unary-position-measures** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-unary-moments)): Fix the integral position supports, the locally constant tests at $p$, and their levels. At a fixed ordinary CM test object, the period-normalized unary jets, in the frames of Convention <a href="#conv:scaled-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="conv:scaled-support">18</a>, are the moments of a bounded measure on the product of their integral position lattices. Its norm is bounded in terms of the coefficients of the fixed tests and their $p$-levels. The bound is independent of the moment degrees and of the moving tame level $q_i$.* *Cutting a position measure to units gives at classical degrees the theta jet with the corresponding unit cut on the finite position test. The corresponding moments converge to these unit moments when every degree tends to infinity and converges on its fixed $p$-adic branch. On a sufficiently narrow branch the unit moments, including the fixed CM unit-scale powers, are analytic functions of the degrees.
  - Explanation: Normalized unary jets define bounded position measures whose bounds survive changing the moving tame level; unit cuts yield analytic degree interpolation on a sufficiently narrow branch.
- **claim-uniform-mixed-expansion-denominators** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mixed-expansions)): The mixed Fourier–Jacobi expansions of <a href="#eq:upper-theta-integral" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:upper-theta-integral">[eq:upper-theta-integral]</a>, in algebraic ordinary differential frames and in the monomial-dual lattice, have a common bounded denominator. The bound is independent of $m$, $i$, and the tame character, and is coefficientwise in the tame group ring. The same assertion holds after substitution into any fixed $R_b$.
  - Explanation: The mixed expansions have uniform coefficientwise denominator bounds in the integral monomial-dual lattice, including after fixed tame truncation.
- **claim-small-constant-terms** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-constant-term-small)): Assume that the conductor-one Heegner point $y$ is torsion. The positive weights $m_i$ in Proposition <a href="#prop:theta-family" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:theta-family">17</a> can be chosen with $m_i\to+\infty$ and $m_i\to0$ on the prescribed $p$-adic branch so that every constant Fourier–Jacobi coefficient of the upper theta form tends coefficientwise to zero modulo $t^{b_*}$. The assertion holds in bounded algebraic ordinary differential bases, uniformly over all boundary representatives and all polynomial components. The weights may be required to satisfy any additional lower bounds and any further $p$-adic approximation conditions tending to zero.
  - Explanation: Under the torsion contradiction hypothesis, weights can be chosen so that all constant coefficients vanish through the required tame order uniformly in boundary representatives.
- **claim-cleared-binary-period-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cleared-period-comparison)): There are group-ring polynomial factors $A_i(m),B_i(m)$ with uniformly bounded coefficients such that $$\label{eq:cleared-period-comparison} A_i(m)S_i(m)^2 =B_i(m)P_{0,i}(m)^2P_{1,i}(m)^2.$$ For each $i$, after taking $m$ sufficiently close to zero on its branch, $A_i(m)$ has nonzero augmentation whose valuation is bounded above by a constant independent of $i$. The required neighborhood of zero may depend on $i$. The identity holds in the full characteristic-zero tame group algebra, including at zeros of the global central $L$-values.
  - Explanation: A cleared algebraic square identity compares the binary theta scalar with raw toric periods, with bounded coefficients and a controlled nonzero augmentation permitting fixed-truncation inversion.
- **claim-positive-index-unfolding** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-one-index-unfolding)): There are finite tests satisfying the $p$- and $q_i$-requirements of Section <a href="#sec:theta" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:theta">4</a> for which the coefficient <a href="#eq:primitive-monomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:primitive-monomial">[eq:primitive-monomial]</a>, at index $(e_1,e_1)$, unfolds into the product of a fixed row-one Heisenberg theta function and $$\label{eq:row-two-unfolding} \int_{[T]}\varphi(s)\eta^{-1}(s) \vartheta_{1,d_1}(sb_1)\vartheta_{2,d_2}(sb_2)\,ds.$$ Here $b_1,b_2\in U(1)(\mathbb A_F)$ translate the binary lines, and known splitting-character factors in $b_j$ have been removed. At $q_i$ the only additional row-two condition is a unit cut in the first binary column. The unfolding introduces a fixed nonzero volume factor and no factor $q_i-1$.
  - Explanation: Permitted local tests unfold a positive-index coefficient into a fixed Heisenberg factor and a stabilizer-torus integral without a growing residue-unit volume factor.
- **claim-simultaneous-unary-nonvanishing** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-simultaneous-unary-nonvanishing)): The fixed finite-order parts of $\xi_1,\xi_2$ can be chosen, preserving <a href="#eq:unary-character-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:unary-character-product">[eq:unary-character-product]</a>, so that suitable permitted local tests give $T_j(d_{j0})\ne0$ at positive classical cut weights $d_{10},d_{20}$. The weights can be chosen so that $d_{10}+d_{20}+1$ is divisible by every prescribed torsion order and a prescribed sufficiently large fixed power of $p$.
  - Explanation: Finite-order character adjustments preserve the character product while making both unary periods nonzero at compatible positive cut weights.
- **claim-bounded-uncontrolled-positive-content** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-uncontrolled-content)): Before imposing the additional fixed-place controls, there are positive $d_{1i},d_{2i}$, with $m_i=d_{1i}+d_{2i}+1$ tending to zero $p$-adically and to infinity archimedeanly, for which the theta forms have a nonconstant Fourier–Jacobi coefficient of valuation bounded above at augmentation. The choices can simultaneously satisfy the constant-term precision of Proposition <a href="#prop:constant-term-small" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:constant-term-small">21</a>.
  - Explanation: Compatible interpolating degree sequences retain a bounded-valuation positive coefficient while meeting the constant-term precision requirements.
- **claim-bounded-fixed-place-spanning** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-place-spanning)): At each such fixed split place, the tests just prescribed and their translates span the tests used in Lemma <a href="#lem:bounded-uncontrolled-content" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bounded-uncontrolled-content">25</a> in theta coinvariants, after inverting a Laurent polynomial nonzero at weight zero. For weights tending to zero, this spanning uses finitely many translates and a common bounded denominator. All replacements can be made independently while leaving the $p$- and $q_i$-tests unchanged.
  - Explanation: At fixed split places, permitted controlled tests span the original tests after a localization that costs only a bounded denominator near weight zero.
- **claim-combined-theta-output** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-bounded-content)): Fix the truncation $b_*$ selected in Section <a href="#sec:tame" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tame">3</a>. There exist positive integers $m_i\to\infty$, tending $p$-adically to zero on the prescribed torsion branch, and theta forms $\Theta_i$ of weight $(m_i,m_i,1;-1)$ with the following simultaneous properties.* 1. *Their ordinary monomial-dual lattices have a uniform denominator bound, coefficientwise in the full tame group algebra and after every fixed truncated substitution used in the argument. One fixed scaling clears this bound.* 2. *All constant Fourier–Jacobi coefficients tend to zero through degree $b_*-1$ of the tame variable, in compatible ordinary differential bases, uniformly at the boundary representatives. This is a full-ring estimate in $R_{b_*}$.* 3. *At augmentation, a nonconstant Fourier–Jacobi coefficient has an integral-frame evaluation of valuation bounded above by a constant independent of $i$. Thus the forms do not acquire unbounded common content.* 4. *At both distinguished places above $p$, the fixed canonical $(2,1,1)$ level is $J$ and the operators $p^{-2}U_2,p^{-2}U_3$ have value $1$. At every controlled upper $(2,2)$ place, including $q_i$, the expanding center is invertible and the last binary spherical data are those of the final pair in <a href="#eq:theta-eigenparameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-eigenparameters">[eq:theta-eigenparameters]</a>. At the specified unramified Steinberg places the level is $(3,1)$ parahoric, without an additional refinement. All nonsplit finite places are hyperspecial.* 5. *At split good spherical places the Hecke parameters are <a href="#eq:theta-eigenparameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta-eigenparameters">[eq:theta-eigenparameters]</a> with $\eta=\mathcal C^{m_i}\nu_i$.* *All constants may depend on the fixed original data, the fixed local tests and $b_*$, but not on $i,q_i,n_i$ or the growing weights.
  - Explanation: One family simultaneously has bounded integral content, vanishing constant terms, a surviving positive coefficient, and all required local Hecke and level controls.
- **claim-canonical-ordinary-branch** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-canonical-ordinary-level)): Over the ordinary formal completion, the locus on which the rank-three flag equals the multiplicative subgroup is an open and closed branch of $Y^{\mathrm{tor}}$. Its residual flag and frame data are finite etale data on the Cartier-dual character group. On a toroidal cusp chart this branch adds finite etale covers of the abelian extension-data scheme and does not ramify the center Fourier variable.
  - Explanation: The multiplicative flag selects a canonical open and closed ordinary branch whose finite etale boundary covers preserve the center Fourier variable.
- **claim-integral-dual-fock-operations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dual-fock-functor)): The functor $\mathcal D_d$ is finite locally free and commutes with base change. Integral changes of Hodge frame preserve its lattice. If a map on $\mathcal H$ is diagonal with entries $c_1,c_2,c_3$, its action on the basis dual to the minor monomials has entries $$(c_1c_2)^{a_{12}}(c_1c_3)^{a_{13}} (c_2c_3)^{a_{23}}, \qquad a_{12}+a_{13}+a_{23}=d.$$ A filtration of $\mathcal H$ by subbundles with constant locally free graded pieces induces such a filtration on $\mathcal D_d(\mathcal H)$.
  - Explanation: The divided-power coefficient functor is integral, finite locally free, compatible with base change, and preserves the filtrations needed at the boundary.
- **claim-cusp-base-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cusp-base-change)): An ordinary section modulo $p^M$ on the canonical branch whose constant terms vanish, extended by zero on the other branches, is a section of $\mathcal M_\lambda/p^M$ over the ordinary open. The assertion remains true with coefficients in $\mathcal O[t]/(p^M,t^b)$ and after finite extension of coefficients.
  - Explanation: Vanishing constant terms on the canonical branch give an ordinary cusp section modulo precision, including over the full truncated coefficient ring.
- **claim-integral-normalized-ordinary-traces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ordinary-trace)): The operators in <a href="#eq:ordinary-normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:ordinary-normalization">[eq:ordinary-normalization]</a> preserve the dual-Fock differential lattice on the canonical ordinary branch and its reductions modulo every power of $p$. After the Hasse shift, both comparison eigenvalues are $1$ modulo the prescribed precision.
  - Explanation: The normalized operators preserve the same integral differential lattice before and after the Hasse shift, retaining the comparison eigenvalues modulo precision.
- **claim-uniform-artin-content-cancellation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-artin-content-cancellation)): Let $K'/k$ be a finite extension, and let $a(t)\in\mathcal O_{K'}[t]/(t^b)$ satisfy $v_p(a(0))\leq C$, where $C$ is a fixed nonnegative integer. If $a(t)r(t)$ belongs to $p^M\mathcal O_{K'}[t]/(t^b)$, then $$r(t)\in p^{M-bC}\mathcal O_{K'}[t]/(t^b).$$ The bound is independent of the extension and of $a$.
  - Explanation: A bounded valuation of the constant coefficient permits cancellation in a fixed truncated ring with a uniform precision loss independent of the coefficient extension.
- **claim-full-ring-classical-cusp-congruence** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-cusp-congruence)): After reducing $M_i$ by a fixed constant and relabeling it, there are homomorphisms of $\mathcal O$-algebras $$\lambda_i^0:T_i^0\longrightarrow (\mathcal O/p^{M_i}\mathcal O)[t]/(t^{b_*}), \qquad M_i\longrightarrow\infty, \label{eq:hecke-congruence}$$ which send every indicated operator to its theta comparison eigenvalue, in the full truncated ring. The weights are <a href="#eq:shifted-weight" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:shifted-weight">[eq:shifted-weight]</a>, and the two normalized $p$-operators have value $1$ at each distinguished place. One may restrict $T_i^0$ to the local summand determined by the residual augmentation system. In that summand both normalized $p$-operators are units. No reduction of the operator algebra is asserted here.
  - Explanation: Bounded positive content forces all classical Hecke relations to hold at the theta comparison values over the entire truncated tame ring, without first reducing the operator algebra.
- **claim-weak-transfer-full-infinite-characters** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-weak-transfer)): There is a conjugate-dual formal parameter $$\Psi=\boxplus_\beta\tau_\beta[a_\beta], \qquad \sum_\beta d_\beta a_\beta=4,$$ with unitary cuspidal factors, the standard transferred full infinitesimal character of $\pi$ at both real places, and the transferred Satake parameter at every finite place outside a finite set $S$ whose finite places split in $L/F$. Every cuspidal factor is unramified at every nonsplit finite place.
  - Explanation: A single conjugate-dual formal parameter matches the full infinite characters and the required finite parameters, with cuspidal factors unramified at nonsplit places.
- **claim-algebraic-transfer-factors** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-transfer-algebraicity)): Every cuspidal factor in <a href="#lem:weak-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:weak-transfer">34</a> is conjugate-self-dual and tempered at infinity. After a unitary parity-correcting character twist it is regular algebraic. The formal parameter is elliptic: its conjugate-self-dual blocks occur without repetition.
  - Explanation: Transferred cuspidal factors are individually conjugate-self-dual and tempered at infinity, admit algebraic parity corrections, and occur without repeated blocks.
- **claim-simultaneous-local-global-transfer** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-simultaneous-transfer)): There is a formal parameter $$\Psi=\boxplus_\beta\tau_\beta[a_\beta], \qquad \sum_\beta d_\beta a_\beta=4,$$ with unitary cuspidal factors on $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_{d_\beta}(\mathbb A_L)$, such that:* 1. *its archimedean infinitesimal character is the standard transfer of that of $\pi$;* 2. *at every split finite place $v=ww^c$, the representation $\pi_v$, viewed on $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_4(F_v)$ using $w$, is the representation attached to $\Psi_w$;* 3. *at every nonsplit finite place, the standard local parameter is unramified and has zero monodromy.
  - Explanation: The same formal parameter supplies the archimedean transfer, all split finite local representations, and unramified nonsplit local parameters with zero monodromy.
- **claim-established-racsd-galois-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-racsd-galois-input)): Let $\Pi$ be a regular algebraic conjugate-self-dual cuspidal representation of $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_d(\mathbb A_L)$ and fix $\iota:\overline{\mathbb Q}_p\simeq\mathbb C$. There is a continuous semisimple representation $r_p(\Pi)$ of $G_L$, with $$r_p(\Pi)^c\simeq r_p(\Pi)^\vee\epsilon^{1-d}.$$ It is de Rham above $p$. If its first infinitesimal coordinates at an embedding are $b_1,\ldots,b_d$, its de Rham weights there are $(d-1)/2-b_j$. At every finite place $w$, $$\iota\mathop{\mathrm{WD}}\bigl(r_p(\Pi)\|_{G_{L_w}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{F\text{-}ss}} \preceq \operatorname{rec}_w \bigl(\Pi_w\otimes\|\det\|_w^{(1-d)/2}\bigr). \label{eq:racsd-compatibility}$$ Here $\preceq$ means equality after semisimplifying the Weil representation and domination of monodromy partitions on each inertial class. At spherical places away from $p$ the representation is unramified, and at spherical places above $p$ it is crystalline.
  - Explanation: The established Galois input attaches a polarized semisimple de Rham representation with specified weights and local compatibility expressed by Weil semisimplification and monodromy domination.
- **claim-cusp-system-galois-package** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-galois-package)): For every characteristic-zero cusp eigensystem in <a href="#prop:cusp-congruence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cusp-congruence">33</a>, there is a continuous semisimple representation $$\rho_\pi:G_L\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_4(\overline{\mathbb Q}_p), \qquad \rho_\pi^c\simeq\rho_\pi^\vee\epsilon^{-3}.$$ It is unramified at every finite place nonsplit over $F$. At every split finite place $v=ww^c$, $$\iota\mathop{\mathrm{WD}}\bigl(\rho_\pi\|_{G_{L_w}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{F\text{-}ss}} \preceq \operatorname{rec}_w \bigl(\pi_v\otimes\|\det\|_w^{-3/2}\bigr). \label{eq:four-dimensional-compatibility}$$ The representation is de Rham above $p$, and its increasing weights at either distinguished $w\mid p$ are $$(h_1,h_2,h_3,h_4) =(1-\lambda'_1,\,2-\lambda'_2,\, 3-\lambda'_3,\,-\lambda'_4). \label{eq:hodge-weights}$$ The gaps $h_3-h_2$ and $h_4-h_3$ tend to infinity with the choices in <a href="#eq:shifted-weight" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:shifted-weight">[eq:shifted-weight]</a>.
  - Explanation: Each characteristic-zero cusp system yields a polarized four-dimensional representation with the prescribed local compatibility and separated de Rham weights.
- **claim-local-hodge-newton-flags** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-two-contacts)): At each distinguished $w\mid p$, every $\rho_\pi$ under consideration has a $G_{L_w}$-stable flag of dimensions $2,3,4$. Its last quotient is $$X_w=\epsilon^{-h_4}\operatorname{unr}(\widetilde U_3^{-1}), \label{eq:last-character}$$ where $\operatorname{unr}(a)$ takes geometric Frobenius to $a$. At the conjugate place it has a first subline of character $$Y_{\bar w}=X_w^{-c}\epsilon^{-3}. \label{eq:first-character}$$ Under <a href="#eq:hecke-congruence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:hecke-congruence">[eq:hecke-congruence]</a> their values tend, uniformly on local Galois elements and coefficientwise in $t$, to $x=\epsilon^{-1}$ and $y=\epsilon^{-2}$, respectively.
  - Explanation: The two unit operators force canonical local flags, a final character quotient and a conjugate initial character line, with uniform full-ring convergence to the comparison characters.
- **claim-separated-binary-inertia** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-binary-inertia)): The augmentation values of $\Delta$ are nonzero with uniformly bounded valuation. On any characteristic-zero system where $\Delta\ne0$, the Frobenius primary projection $P_v^{\mathrm{bin}}$ onto the roots of $A_v$ is defined and satisfies $$(\rho_\pi(\iota)-1)P_v^{\mathrm{bin}}=0 \qquad(\iota\in I_{L_v}). \label{eq:binary-inertia-identity}$$ The assertion concerns actual inertia, without a semisimplicity assumption on Frobenius.
  - Explanation: Uniformly controlled resultants separate the binary allocation, on which actual inertia is trivial even without semisimple Frobenius.
- **claim-steinberg-inertia-rank** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-steinberg-control)): At a split place with the specified $(3,1)$ parahoric level, $\rho_\pi\|_I$ is unipotent and $$\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}(\rho_\pi(\iota)-1)\leq1\qquad(\iota\in I).$$
  - Explanation: The prescribed parahoric level forces unipotent inertia with rank at most one for the inertia difference.
- **claim-uniform-operator-nilpotent-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-nilpotent-control)): Let $T_i$ be the image of $T_i^0$ in its characteristic-zero cusp eigensystems. There is an integer $C$, independent of $i$, the weights and $q_i$, such that $$\Delta^C\ker(T_i^0\longrightarrow T_i)=0. \label{eq:nilpotent-annihilation}$$ After decreasing the precisions by a fixed constant, <a href="#eq:hecke-congruence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:hecke-congruence">[eq:hecke-congruence]</a> descends to $$\lambda_i:T_i\longrightarrow \mathcal O/(p^{M_i})[t]/(t^{b_*}),\qquad M_i\longrightarrow\infty. \label{eq:reduced-hecke-congruence}$$
  - Explanation: A uniform power of the separation resultant annihilates the kernel of characteristic-zero eigensystem detection, allowing congruence descent with bounded precision loss.
- **claim-uniform-matrix-algebra-generators** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-algebra-generators)): There is an integer $N$ independent of $i$ such that the $T_i$-module $B_i$ is generated by $N$ monomials in images of Galois elements.
  - Explanation: The matrix algebra admits a uniformly bounded number of module generators drawn from Galois monomials despite growing coefficient orders and tame levels.
- **claim-finite-value-admissibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-value-admissibility)): If $\Phi:B\to M_4(R_b)$ is a $T$-linear algebra homomorphism with scalar map $\lambda$, then $\Phi\rho_*$ has admissible matrix coefficients. The same holds with $k$ in place of $R_b$. Algebraic local identities holding on all local sequences have the uniform increasing-precision meaning in <a href="#def:admissible" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:admissible">8</a>.
  - Explanation: An algebra homomorphism to the fixed truncated matrix ring yields admissible coefficients, and identities on all local sequences have uniform increasing-precision representatives.
- **claim-intrinsic-corner-minors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-corner-minors)): Choose four rows of a stack of $4\times4$ matrices in $B$, with column indices $1,2,x,y$. If the row indices consist of the column indices with $y$ replaced by $u$, then its signed determinant is a well-defined element of $D_{uy}$. The same assertion holds for a square minor whenever canceling common row and column indices leaves precisely one surplus row and one surplus column; if no indices remain, its value lies in $T$. If the ordinary determinant is zero in every ambient field, this corner element is zero. Its identity is preserved by algebra maps out of $B$.
  - Explanation: Suitable determinants define intrinsic corner elements whose ambient-field vanishing and algebra-map compatibility transport rank conditions to triangular quotients.
- **claim-cyclotomic-corner-factorization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-cyclotomic-corner)): The corner modules satisfy $$D_{xy}=D_{xf}D_{fy}. \label{eq:xy-factorization}$$
  - Explanation: The unwanted character-to-character corner factors through the elliptic block because its remaining strict cyclotomic extension quotient vanishes.
- **claim-no-stable-last-line** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-no-stable-y-line)): The image of $D_{fy}$ is nonzero at every field evaluation of $\mathcal R$.
  - Explanation: The elliptic-to-last-character corner survives every ambient field evaluation; a vanishing corner would force incompatible geometric and residual weight sums.
- **claim-vanishing-corner-fitting-ideal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-fitting-vanishing)): One has $$\mathop{\mathrm{Fitt}}_{0,T}(D_{fy})=0. \label{eq:fitting-vanishing}$$
  - Explanation: Nonvanishing at every ambient field and reducedness force the corner annihilator, and hence its zeroth Fitting ideal, to vanish.
- **claim-greenberg-extension-extraction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-extension-extraction)): There is an injective $R_b$-linear map $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R_b}(D_{fy}\otimes_{T,\lambda}R_b,R_b) \hookrightarrow H^1_{\mathrm{Gr}}(L,V\otimes\psi^{\pm1}). \label{eq:green-injection}$$ Its source has $R_b$-length at least $b$.
  - Explanation: Duals of the base-changed corner inject into the admissible one-sided Greenberg group and have length at least the truncation degree, contradicting the upper bound.
- **claim-sylvester-positive-prime-cases** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-sylvester)): Let $\ell$ be a rational prime with $\ell\equiv4,7,8\pmod 9$, and equip the projective cubic $$E_\ell:\quad X^3+Y^3=\ell Z^3$$ with origin $(1:-1:0)$. Then $$\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(E_\ell,s)=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_\mathbb ZE_\ell(\mathbb Q)=1, \qquad \#\operatorname{Sha}(E_\ell/\mathbb Q)<\infty.$$ In particular, there are $x,y\in\mathbb Q$ such that $x^3+y^3=\ell$.
  - Explanation: For the specified prime residue classes, the cubic has analytic and Mordell–Weil rank one and finite Tate–Shafarevich group, implying rational cube-sum solvability.
- **claim-normalized-global-torus-factor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-global-torus-factor)): Let $K=\mathbb F_q(X)$, where $X$ is a smooth projective geometrically connected curve of genus $g$, and let $\Gamma_K$ be its absolute Galois group. Let $T_1,T_2$ be $K$-tori with isomorphic rational character modules as Galois representations, and let $\ell:\operatorname{Lie}T_1\simeq\operatorname{Lie}T_2$ be a $K$-linear isomorphism. Choose local Haar measures $\eta_{i,v}$ compatible under $\ell$ on the Lie algebras, and give the smooth integral compact subgroups volume one at almost all places. Write $\eta_i$ for the resulting adelic measures, using counting measure on $T_i(K)$.* *For a torus $T$, put $Y=X^*(T)$, $r=\operatorname{rank}Y^{\Gamma_K}$, and $$\deg_T(t)(\chi)=\sum_v [k(v):\mathbb F_q]\, v\bigl(\chi(t_v)\bigr),\qquad I(T)=\bigl[\operatorname{Hom}(Y^{\Gamma_K},\mathbb Z): \deg_T T(\mathbb A_K)\bigr].$$ Let $T(\mathbb A_K)^1=\ker\deg_T$, and set $$\operatorname{Sha}^1(K,T)=\ker\!\left(H^1(K,T)\longrightarrow \prod_v H^1(K_v,T)\right), \qquad b(T)=\bigl\|\pi_0(\widehat T^{\Gamma_K})\bigr\|.$$ If $$c_{\eta}(T)= \operatorname{vol}_{\eta}\bigl(T(K)\backslash T(\mathbb A_K)^1\bigr) \,\|\operatorname{Sha}^1(K,T)\|,$$ then $$\label{eq:torus-factor-degree-index} \frac{c_{\eta_1}(T_1)/b(T_1)}{c_{\eta_2}(T_2)/b(T_2)} =\frac{I(T_1)}{I(T_2)}.$$ In particular the two normalized factors agree if each torus splits at a place of degree one. The places may be different.
  - Explanation: Compatible measures and rationally isomorphic character modules make the ratio of normalized torus factors equal the degree-index ratio; split degree-one places remove that remaining discrepancy.
- **claim-nonstandard-change-of-characteristic** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-nonstandard-change-characteristic)): Fix a finite collection of paired based root data with rational root-ray maps $\psi$, matching Levi data, and compatible finite cyclic outer actions. Suppose the nonstandard weighted fundamental lemma holds over local fields of sufficiently large positive characteristic for their unramified forms, including all cyclic-generator variants. Then it holds over characteristic-zero local fields of sufficiently large residue characteristic, uniformly for this collection and for all integral generically regular matching parameters.* *Here the ambient hyperspecial subgroups have volume one; the Arthur weight-space measures are transported through $\psi$; and the paired regular-centralizer measures are transported through the induced Lie-algebra isomorphism.
  - Explanation: For fixed paired root data and compatible measures, the positive-characteristic nonstandard identity transfers uniformly to characteristic-zero fields with sufficiently large residue characteristic.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-auxiliary-fields",
        "exact_quote": "There are coprime negative odd fundamental discriminants $D,D'$ such that every prime dividing $2Np$ splits in both quadratic fields, $$\\left(\\frac{D}{|D'|}\\right)=1,$$ and each of $A_0^D,A_0^{D'},A_0^{DD'}$ has the smallest analytic rank permitted by its sign. They may also be chosen so that $$K=\\mathbb Q(\\sqrt D),\\qquad F=\\mathbb Q(\\sqrt{DD'}),\\qquad\n L=\\mathbb Q(\\sqrt D,\\sqrt{D'})$$ satisfy the following properties: $L/F$ is CM and unramified at every finite prime, $L$ has only the roots of unity $\\{\\pm1\\}$, and $L$ does not contain the CM field of $A_0$ if $A_0$ has CM.",
        "source_id": "PM-002.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-auxiliary-fields",

          "source_id": "PM-002.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-arithmetic-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "With these choices, $$\\dim H^1_f(K,V)=1,\n \\qquad\n \\dim H^1_f(K,V^{DD'})=1.$$ The second line localizes nontrivially at both places of $K$ over $p$. Let $y\\in A_0(K)$ be the norm of the conductor-one Heegner point under a modular parametrization taking the cusp $\\infty$ to zero.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-base-green",
        "exact_quote": "If the compact Selmer line of $W/K$ has nonzero localization, then $H^1_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}}(K,W)=H^1_{\\overline{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}}}(K,W)=0$. The only remaining possibility is the following *strict case* in the $V$ summand: $r=1$, and the compact Selmer space is generated by a class $a$ such that $a^c=a$ and every localization of $a$ is zero. In that case $$\\label{eq:base-green-bases}\n H^1_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}}(K,V)=\\langle a,h\\rangle,\n \\qquad\n H^1_{\\overline{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}}}(K,V)=\\langle a,h^c\\rangle,$$ where $h$ has nonzero localization at $v$ and zero localization at $v^c$. The $V^{DD'}$ summand always has zero Greenberg space.",
        "source_id": "PM-002.2"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-base-green",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-tame-primes",
        "exact_quote": "There are rational primes $q_i\\notin S$, split completely in $L$, distinguished places $\\mathfrak q_i$ of $K$ above them, cyclic $p$-quotients $\\Delta_i$ of the ring class group of conductor $q_i$, and surjections $$\\ell_i:G_K\\longrightarrow\\Delta_i\\simeq\\mathbb Z/p^{n_i}\\mathbb Z,\n \\qquad n_i\\longrightarrow\\infty,$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *$\\ell_i$ is unramified outside the pair above $q_i$, its restriction to inertia at $\\mathfrak q_i$ is surjective, $\\ell_i^c=-\\ell_i$, and $\\ell_i|_{G_{K_s}}=0$ for every $s\\in S$.*\n\n2.  *$v_p(q_i-1)-n_i$ is bounded. At either place above $p$, the full ring class field of conductor $q_i$ is unramified and its local degree has bounded $p$-part.*\n\n3.  *Frobenius at $\\mathfrak q_i$, on all fixed data unramified outside $S$, tends to an element $\\gamma\\in G_L$ such that $$\\epsilon(\\gamma)=1,\\qquad\n     \\det(V(\\gamma)-1)\\ne0,\\qquad\n     \\mathcal C(\\gamma)\\text{ is not a root of unity}.$$ Moreover $\\gamma$ fixes all $p$-power radicals of a fixed, conjugation-stable finite list in $K^\\times$ spanning $\\mathcal O_{K,S}^{\\times}\\otimes\\mathbb Q_p$ under the Kummer map.",
        "source_id": "PM-002.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tame-primes",

At either place above $p$, the full ring class field of conductor $q_i$ is unramified and its local degree has bounded $p$-part.*\n\n3.  *Frobenius at $\\mathfrak q_i$, on all fixed data unramified outside $S$, tends to an element $\\gamma\\in G_L$ such that $$\\epsilon(\\gamma)=1,\\qquad\n     \\det(V(\\gamma)-1)\\ne0,\\qquad\n     \\mathcal C(\\gamma)\\text{ is not a root of unity}.$$ Moreover $\\gamma$ fixes all $p$-power radicals of a fixed, conjugation-stable finite list in $K^\\times$ spanning $\\mathcal O_{K,S}^{\\times}\\otimes\\mathbb Q_p$ under the Kummer map.",
          "source_id": "PM-002.2"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-admissible-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "For constant coefficients $V$, admissible $H^1$ is precisely $H^1(G_{K,S},V)$, and likewise over $L$. For $\\mathbb Q_p(1)$ the same assertion holds when unramifiedness at the moving primes is imposed. The fixed local conditions specialize to the usual rational local conditions. Coefficient reduction and multiplication by $t$ give short exact sequences of admissible cochain complexes $$\\label{eq:coefficient-sequence}\n 0\\longrightarrow C^\\bullet(M_{b-1}^{\\eta})\n \\xrightarrow{\\ t\\ } C^\\bullet(M_b^{\\eta})\n \\longrightarrow C^\\bullet(V\\otimes k)\\longrightarrow0.$$ Zero local conditions at $p$ and local Kummer conditions are preserved by the resulting division of a class with zero specialization by $t$.",
        "source_id": "PM-002.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-nonzero-obstruction",
        "exact_quote": "The element $\\gamma$ in <a href=\"#prop:tame-primes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:tame-primes\">7</a> can be chosen so that, in the bases of <a href=\"#eq:base-green-bases\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:base-green-bases\">[eq:base-green-bases]</a>, the obstruction matrix is $$\\label{eq:obstruction-matrix}\n \\begin{pmatrix}0&\\kappa\\\\-\\kappa&0\\end{pmatrix},\n \\qquad \\kappa\\in\\mathbb Q_p^\\times.$$ Thus no nonzero base Greenberg class lifts to order two.",

          "exact_quote": "The element $\\gamma$ in <a href=\"#prop:tame-primes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:tame-primes\">7</a> can be chosen so that, in the bases of <a href=\"#eq:base-green-bases\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:base-green-bases\">[eq:base-green-bases]</a>, the obstruction matrix is $$\\label{eq:obstruction-matrix}\n \\begin{pmatrix}0&\\kappa\\\\-\\kappa&0\\end{pmatrix},\n \\qquad \\kappa\\in\\mathbb Q_p^\\times.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-002.2"
        }

      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-nonzero-obstruction"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-green-length",
        "exact_quote": "For the primes of <a href=\"#prop:tame-primes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:tame-primes\">7</a>, chosen in the strict case as in <a href=\"#lem:nonzero-obstruction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:nonzero-obstruction\">11</a>, every fixed $b\\ge1$, and either $\\eta=1$ or $\\eta=-1$, one has $$\\label{eq:green-length}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{length}}_{R_b}H^1_{\\mathrm{Gr}}(L,V\\otimes\\psi^{\\eta})\n \\begin{cases}\n =0,&\\text{outside the strict case},\\\\\n \\le2,&\\text{in the strict case}.\n \\end{cases}$$ In the strict case this module is killed by $t$ and is the image of its two-dimensional base space under multiplication by $t^{b-1}$. Put $$\\label{eq:b-star}\n b_*=1\\quad\\text{outside the strict case},\\qquad\n b_*=3\\quad\\text{in the strict case}.$$ Thus in both cases the length in <a href=\"#eq:green-length\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:green-length\">[eq:green-length]</a> is strictly smaller than $b_*$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-uniform-local-log",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $d_0\\ge0$. There is a constant $c$ such that, for every finite unramified extension $E/\\mathbb Q_p$ with $v_p([E:\\mathbb Q_p])\\le d_0$, $$\\label{eq:uniform-local-log}\n \\log_{A_0,p}(A_0(E))\\subseteq p^{-c}\\mathcal O_E.$$ If $P\\in A_0(E)$ and the Kummer class of $p^C P$ vanishes in $H^1(E,A_0[p^n])$, where $C,n\\ge0$ are integers, then $$v_p(\\log_{A_0,p}P)\\ge n-C-c.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-002.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $d_0\\ge0$.         }

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-heegner-log",
        "exact_quote": "At the distinguished place over $p$, and for either sign, $$\\label{eq:heegner-log}\n \\sum_{g\\in\\mathcal G_i}(1+t)^{\\pm\\ell_i(g)}\n       \\log_{A_0,p}(y_i^g)\n       \\longrightarrow0\\pmod {t^{b_*}}.$$ More precisely, every coefficient of degree less than $b_*$ has valuation tending to $+\\infty$ along $\\mathcal U$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-theta-family",
        "exact_quote": "For the characters <a href=\"#eq:theta-character\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-character\">[eq:theta-character]</a> and every sufficiently large positive $m$ on the prescribed branch, the theta construction gives holomorphic algebraic forms $\\Theta_{i,m}$ of weight <a href=\"#eq:theta-weight\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-weight\">[eq:theta-weight]</a>. In the canonical ordinary monomial-dual lattice their coefficients have a common bounded denominator, coefficientwise in the tame group algebra and in every fixed truncated substitution $R_b$. They have spherical parameters <a href=\"#eq:theta-eigenparameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-eigenparameters\">[eq:theta-eigenparameters]</a>, and at both distinguished $p$-places they satisfy $$p^{-2}U_2\\Theta_{i,m}=\\Theta_{i,m},\\qquad\n  p^{-2}U_3\\Theta_{i,m}=\\Theta_{i,m}.$$ These assertions allow arbitrary fixed split tests of the stipulated bounded levels. They remain valid for the particular tests with all the finite local controls in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:bounded-content\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:bounded-content\">27</a>.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-unary-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the integral position supports, the locally constant tests at $p$, and their levels. At a fixed ordinary CM test object, the period-normalized unary jets, in the frames of Convention <a href=\"#conv:scaled-support\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"conv:scaled-support\">18</a>, are the moments of a bounded measure on the product of their integral position lattices. Its norm is bounded in terms of the coefficients of the fixed tests and their $p$-levels. The bound is independent of the moment degrees and of the moving tame level $q_i$.*\n\n*Cutting a position measure to units gives at classical degrees the theta jet with the corresponding unit cut on the finite position test. The corresponding moments converge to these unit moments when every degree tends to infinity and converges on its fixed $p$-adic branch. On a sufficiently narrow branch the unit moments, including the fixed CM unit-scale powers, are analytic functions of the degrees.",
        "source_id": "PM-002.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-mixed-expansions",
        "exact_quote": "The mixed Fourier–Jacobi expansions of <a href=\"#eq:upper-theta-integral\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:upper-theta-integral\">[eq:upper-theta-integral]</a>, in algebraic ordinary differential frames and in the monomial-dual lattice, have a common bounded denominator. The bound is independent of $m$, $i$, and the tame character, and is coefficientwise in the tame group ring. The same assertion holds after substitution into any fixed $R_b$.",
        "source_id": "PM-002.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-constant-term-small",
        "exact_quote": "Assume that the conductor-one Heegner point $y$ is torsion. The assertion holds in bounded algebraic ordinary differential bases, uniformly over all boundary representatives and all polynomial components.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-bounded-content",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the truncation $b_*$ selected in Section <a href=\"#sec:tame\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:tame\">3</a>. There exist positive integers $m_i\\to\\infty$, tending $p$-adically to zero on the prescribed torsion branch, and theta forms $\\Theta_i$ of weight $(m_i,m_i,1;-1)$ with the following simultaneous properties.*\n\n1.  *Their ordinary monomial-dual lattices have a uniform denominator bound, coefficientwise in the full tame group algebra and after every fixed truncated substitution used in the argument. One fixed scaling clears this bound.*\n\n2.  *All constant Fourier–Jacobi coefficients tend to zero through degree $b_*-1$ of the tame variable, in compatible ordinary differential bases, uniformly at the boundary representatives. This is a full-ring estimate in $R_{b_*}$.*\n\n3.  *At augmentation, a nonconstant Fourier–Jacobi coefficient has an integral-frame evaluation of valuation bounded above by a constant independent of $i$. Thus the forms do not acquire unbounded common content.*\n\n4.  *At both distinguished places above $p$, the fixed canonical $(2,1,1)$ level is $J$ and the operators $p^{-2}U_2,p^{-2}U_3$ have value $1$. At every controlled upper $(2,2)$ place, including $q_i$, the expanding center is invertible and the last binary spherical data are those of the final pair in <a href=\"#eq:theta-eigenparameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-eigenparameters\">[eq:theta-eigenparameters]</a>. At the specified unramified Steinberg places the level is $(3,1)$ parahoric, without an additional refinement. All nonsplit finite places are hyperspecial.*\n\n5.  *At split good spherical places the Hecke parameters are <a href=\"#eq:theta-eigenparameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-eigenparameters\">[eq:theta-eigenparameters]</a> with $\\eta=\\mathcal C^{m_i}\\nu_i$.*\n\n*All constants may depend on the fixed original data, the fixed local tests and $b_*$, but not on $i,q_i,n_i$ or the growing weights.",
        "source_id": "PM-002.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-bounded-content",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the truncation $b_*$ selected in Section <a href=\"#sec:tame\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:tame\">3</a>. There exist positive integers $m_i\\to\\infty$, tending $p$-adically to zero on the prescribed torsion branch, and theta forms $\\Theta_i$ of weight $(m_i,m_i,1;-1)$ with the following simultaneous properties.*\n\n1.  *Their ordinary monomial-dual lattices have a uniform denominator bound, coefficientwise in the full tame group algebra and after every fixed truncated substitution used in the argument. One fixed scaling clears this bound.*\n\n2.  *All constant Fourier–Jacobi coefficients tend to zero through degree $b_*-1$ of the tame variable, in compatible ordinary differential bases, uniformly at the boundary representatives. This is a full-ring estimate in $R_{b_*}$.*\n\n3.  *At augmentation, a nonconstant Fourier–Jacobi coefficient has an integral-frame evaluation of valuation bounded above by a constant independent of $i$. Thus the forms do not acquire unbounded common content.*\n\n4.  *At both distinguished places above $p$, the fixed canonical $(2,1,1)$ level is $J$ and the operators $p^{-2}U_2,p^{-2}U_3$ have value $1$. At every controlled upper $(2,2)$ place, including $q_i$, the expanding center is invertible and the last binary spherical data are those of the final pair in <a href=\"#eq:theta-eigenparameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-eigenparameters\">[eq:theta-eigenparameters]</a>. At the specified unramified Steinberg places the level is $(3,1)$ parahoric, without an additional refinement. All nonsplit finite places are hyperspecial.*\n\n5.  *At split good spherical places the Hecke parameters are <a href=\"#eq:theta-eigenparameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-eigenparameters\">[eq:theta-eigenparameters]</a> with $\\eta=\\mathcal C^{m_i}\\nu_i$.*\n\n*All constants may depend on the fixed original data, the fixed local tests and $b_*$, but not on $i,q_i,n_i$ or the growing weights.",
          "source_id": "PM-002.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix the truncation $b_*$ selected in Section <a href=\"#sec:tame\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:tame\">3</a>. There exist positive integers $m_i\\to\\infty$, tending $p$-adically to zero on the prescribed torsion branch, and theta forms $\\Theta_i$ of weight $(m_i,m_i,1;-1)$ with the following simultaneous properties.*\n\n1.  *Their ordinary monomial-dual lattices have a uniform denominator bound, coefficientwise in the full tame group algebra and after every fixed truncated substitution used in the argument. One fixed scaling clears this bound.*\n\n2.  *All constant Fourier–Jacobi coefficients tend to zero through degree $b_*-1$ of the tame variable, in compatible ordinary differential bases, uniformly at the boundary representatives. This is a full-ring estimate in $R_{b_*}$.*\n\n3.  *At augmentation, a nonconstant Fourier–Jacobi coefficient has an integral-frame evaluation of valuation bounded above by a constant independent of $i$. Thus the forms do not acquire unbounded common content.*\n\n4.  *At both distinguished places above $p$, the fixed canonical $(2,1,1)$ level is $J$ and the operators $p^{-2}U_2,p^{-2}U_3$ have value $1$. At every controlled upper $(2,2)$ place, including $q_i$, the expanding center is invertible and the last binary spherical data are those of the final pair in <a href=\"#eq:theta-eigenparameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-eigenparameters\">[eq:theta-eigenparameters]</a>. At the specified unramified Steinberg places the level is $(3,1)$ parahoric, without an additional refinement. All nonsplit finite places are hyperspecial.*\n\n5.  *At split good spherical places the Hecke parameters are <a href=\"#eq:theta-eigenparameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-eigenparameters\">[eq:theta-eigenparameters]</a> with $\\eta=\\mathcal C^{m_i}\\nu_i$.*\n\n*All constants may depend on the fixed original data, the fixed local tests and $b_*$, but not on $i,q_i,n_i$ or the growing weights.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-canonical-ordinary-level",
        "exact_quote": "Over the ordinary formal completion, the locus on which the rank-three flag equals the multiplicative subgroup is an open and closed branch of $Y^{\\mathrm{tor}}$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-dual-fock-functor",
        "exact_quote": "The functor $\\mathcal D_d$ is finite locally free and commutes with base change. Integral changes of Hodge frame preserve its lattice.       "exact_statement": "There is a formal parameter $$\\Psi=\\boxplus_\\beta\\tau_\\beta[a_\\beta],\n \\qquad \\sum_\\beta d_\\beta a_\\beta=4,$$ with unitary cuspidal factors on $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_{d_\\beta}(\\mathbb A_L)$, such that:*\n\n1.  *its archimedean infinitesimal character is the standard transfer of that of $\\pi$;*\n\n2.  *at every split finite place $v=ww^c$, the representation $\\pi_v$, viewed on $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_4(F_v)$ using $w$, is the representation attached to $\\Psi_w$;*\n\n3.  *at every nonsplit finite place, the standard local parameter is unramified and has zero monodromy.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-racsd-galois-input",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Pi$ be a regular algebraic conjugate-self-dual cuspidal representation of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_d(\\mathbb A_L)$ and fix $\\iota:\\overline{\\mathbb Q}_p\\simeq\\mathbb C$. There is a continuous semisimple representation $r_p(\\Pi)$ of $G_L$, with $$r_p(\\Pi)^c\\simeq r_p(\\Pi)^\\vee\\epsilon^{1-d}.$$ It is de Rham above $p$. If its first infinitesimal coordinates at an embedding are $b_1,\\ldots,b_d$, its de Rham weights there are $(d-1)/2-b_j$. At every finite place $w$, $$\\iota\\mathop{\\mathrm{WD}}\\bigl(r_p(\\Pi)|_{G_{L_w}}\\bigr)^{\\mathrm{F\\text{-}ss}}\n \\preceq\n \\operatorname{rec}_w\n     \\bigl(\\Pi_w\\otimes|\\det|_w^{(1-d)/2}\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:racsd-compatibility}$$ Here $\\preceq$ means equality after semisimplifying the Weil representation and domination of monodromy partitions on each inertial class. At spherical places away from $p$ the representation is unramified, and at spherical places above $p$ it is crystalline.",
        "source_id": "PM-002.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "For every characteristic-zero cusp eigensystem in <a href=\"#prop:cusp-congruence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cusp-congruence\">33</a>, there is a continuous semisimple representation $$\\rho_\\pi:G_L\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_4(\\overline{\\mathbb Q}_p),\n \\qquad\n \\rho_\\pi^c\\simeq\\rho_\\pi^\\vee\\epsilon^{-3}.$$ It is unramified at every finite place nonsplit over $F$.         "source_id": "PM-002.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $T_i$ be the image of $T_i^0$ in its characteristic-zero cusp eigensystems.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-corner-minors",
        "exact_quote": "Choose four rows of a stack of $4\\times4$ matrices in $B$, with column indices $1,2,x,y$. If the row indices consist of the column indices with $y$ replaced by $u$, then its signed determinant is a well-defined element of $D_{uy}$. The same assertion holds for a square minor whenever canceling common row and column indices leaves precisely one surplus row and one surplus column; if no indices remain, its value lies in $T$. If the ordinary determinant is zero in every ambient field, this corner element is zero. Its identity is preserved by algebra maps out of $B$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Tate module and rational Tate representation** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For a number field $M$, put $T_pA_0=\varprojlim_n A_0[p^n]$ and $V_pA_0=T_pA_0\otimes_{\mathbb Z_p}\mathbb Q_p$.
- **Tate–Shafarevich group** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $\operatorname{Sha}(A_0/\mathbb Q)$ for the kernel of localization from the Weil–Châtelet group to the product of the local Weil–Châtelet groups.
- **Admissible cochain** ([source](#stmt-def-admissible)): An *admissible cochain* with coefficients in $M_b^{\eta}$ is a function on sequences of Galois elements which is the coefficientwise ultralimit of continuous finite-stage cochains, in the fixed basis of $V\otimes R_b$, with one denominator $p^C$ independent of $i$.
- **Position-moment frame convention** ([source](#stmt-conv-scaled-support)): The position-moment functional is defined first in fixed Igusa differential frames.
- **Uniform valuation bound** ([source](#sec:conventions)): A *uniform* bound may depend on the fixed data and on a fixed truncation degree, but not on the moving prime, its growing $p$-power quotient, or the growing weights.
- **Raw toric sum** ([source](#sec:conventions)): All toric sums called *raw* below are sums without division by the varying ring-class order.
- **Geometric reciprocity and de Rham weight convention** ([source](#sec:conventions)): A de Rham weight of $\epsilon^{-1}$ is called $1$.
- **One-sided Greenberg group** ([source](#pm-h-5)): The one-sided Greenberg group $\mathcal H_b=H^1_{\mathrm{Gr}}(L,V\otimes\psi)$ allows unrestricted local classes at the chosen places and requires zero classes at their conjugates, with relaxed conditions at the remaining fixed places.
- **Strict case** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Otherwise that line is everywhere locally zero; we call this the strict case.

| q-greenberg-length-strict | Admissible Greenberg module length in the strict case | ≤ | $2$ | Uniform in the fixed truncation degree and either tame sign. |
| q-critical-truncation-nonstrict | Critical tame truncation outside the strict case | = | $1$ | The nonzero-localization alternative. |
| q-critical-truncation-strict | Critical tame truncation in the strict case | = | $3$ | The everywhere locally zero compact Selmer line. |
| q-split-parabolic-operator-value | Eigenvalue of either unnormalized pivot-test parabolic operator | = | $p^2$ | The permitted pivot tests of fixed depth. |
| q-artin-cancellation-precision | Coefficientwise divisibility exponent after cancellation | ≥ | $M-bC$ | A fixed truncation and fixed constant-coefficient valuation bound. |
| q-steinberg-inertia-rank | Rank of inertia minus the identity | ≤ | $1$ | Every inertia element at the specified parahoric level. |
| q-extracted-extension-length | Length of the dual base-changed extension corner | ≥ | $b$ | The critical fixed truncation used in the contradiction. |
| q-sylvester-common-rank | Analytic and Mordell–Weil rank of the specified prime cubic | = | $1$ | Rational primes in the displayed residue classes. |

            "anchor": "eq:p-pivot-test",
            "exact_quote": "Order the distinguished columns at $p$ as $(e,B_1,B_2,f)$. The $e$ direction is multiplicative. Our tests have the form $$\\label{eq:p-pivot-test}\n \\Phi_p(Y)=\\phi_p(Y_{[1,2]})\n           \\mathbf 1_{\\mathbb Z_p^2}(Y_3)\\mathbf 1_{\\mathbb Z_p^2}(Y_4),\n \\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\phi_p\\subset\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_2(\\mathbb Z_p),$$ where $\\phi_p$ is locally constant at a fixed depth $s$. They are fixed by a subgroup $J\\subset\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_4(\\mathbb Z_p)$ with upper block pattern $(2,1,1)$: its upper block unipotent is full, its first Levi is a principal congruence subgroup of depth $s$, its two remaining unit factors are full, and its lower block entries have depth $s$. Increasing the fixed $s$ if needed makes the same description work for all the tests. On the canonical component the rank-three distinguished flag member modulo $p^s$ is the multiplicative subgroup. This level records only flags and frames in that subgroup; it does not choose a splitting of the étale quotient. Its integral geometric description will be given in Section <a href=\"#sec:cusp-lifting\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:cusp-lifting\">7</a>.",
            "source_id": "PM-002.2"

If $a(t)r(t)$ belongs to $p^M\\mathcal O_{K'}[t]/(t^b)$, then $$r(t)\\in p^{M-bC}\\mathcal O_{K'}[t]/(t^b).$$ The bound is independent of the extension and of $a$.",
