<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-even-prime-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-two-converse-input) / [proof](#sec:reduction): Apply the established even-prime converse. -> The main conclusion at the even prime.
- **step-density-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-twist-density-input) / [proof](#sec:reduction): Use density-zero exceptional sets inside fixed positive-density congruence classes. -> Availability of auxiliary twists with minimal analytic rank.
- **step-auxiliary-fields** [claim](#stmt-lem-auxiliary-fields) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-auxiliary-fields): Impose congruences, exclude forbidden fields, and apply twist density before comparing discriminants. -> Compatible imaginary quadratic and biquadratic CM fields.
- **step-arithmetic-reduction** [claim](#stmt-prop-arithmetic-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-arithmetic-reduction): Combine root signs, quadratic restriction and corestriction, and the analytic rank-zero and rank-one theorems. -> Compact Selmer lines and reduction to Heegner non-torsion.
- **step-base-greenberg** [claim](#stmt-lem-base-green) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-base-green): Apply Poitou–Tate duality and conjugation symmetry to the local hyperbolic planes. -> The vanishing-or-strict Greenberg dichotomy.
- **step-tame-primes** [claim](#stmt-prop-tame-primes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tame-primes): Use ring-class presentations, radical splitting, Lie-image separation, and Chebotarev. -> Controlled growing tame quotients and limiting Frobenius.
- **step-admissible-specialization** [claim](#stmt-lem-admissible-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-admissible-specialization): Kill moving inertia with uniformly invertible matrices and take compact limits of finite cocycle sets. -> Ordinary constant-coefficient cohomology and exact coefficient division.
- **step-obstruction-necessary** [claim](#stmt-lem-obstruction-necessary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-obstruction-necessary): Pair the first-order lifting equation with opposite local conditions and sum tame local invariants. -> A necessary vanishing condition for first-order lifts.
- **step-obstruction-nondegenerate** [claim](#stmt-lem-nonzero-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nonzero-obstruction): Modify the limiting Frobenius by a Heisenberg commutator preserving all fixed character and radical values. -> Nondegenerate obstruction and refined tame sequence.
- **step-greenberg-upper-length** [claim](#stmt-prop-green-length) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-green-length): Iterate the exact coefficient sequence on the strict-local mapping cone. -> A uniform upper length smaller than the critical truncation.
- **step-uniform-local-log** [claim](#stmt-lem-uniform-local-log) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniform-local-log): Control formal-group and reduction quotients uniformly under the permitted unramified extensions. -> Uniform logarithm denominators and precision comparison.
- **step-heegner-logarithms** [claim](#stmt-prop-heegner-log) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-heegner-log): Corestrict integral Kummer cocycles, apply the Heegner norm relation, divide tame coefficients, and use conjugation. -> Weighted logarithm vanishing through the critical tame order.
- **step-split-parabolic-values** [claim](#stmt-lem-split-u-operators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-split-u-operators): Count surviving upper shifts on the invertible pivot support. -> Exact eigenvalues for both normalized unit operators.
- **step-spherical-theta-parameter** [claim](#stmt-lem-theta-eigenparameters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-theta-eigenparameters): Read the matched unramified theta correspondence and open rank-two orbit. -> The elliptic block and final binary parameter pair.
- **step-unary-moment-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-unary-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-unary-moments): Algebraize scalar squares in a common Igusa ring, bound compatible cusp expansions, and remove vertical denominators. -> Bounded unary position measures and analytic unit moments.
- **step-mixed-expansion-integrality** [claim](#stmt-lem-mixed-expansions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mixed-expansions): Decompose scalar coefficient tests into unary tensors and detect integrality by dense prime-to-prime torsion evaluations. -> Uniform mixed expansion denominators in fixed truncations.
- **step-theta-family** [claim](#stmt-prop-theta-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mixed-expansions-2): Assemble the Fock weight calculation, mixed expansions, spherical parameters, and local operator identities. -> The algebraic holomorphic theta family with all stated integral controls.
- **step-cleared-period-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-cleared-period-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cleared-period-comparison): Match bilinear theta and toric period formulas, cancel local functional-equation factors, and clear moving Euler denominators. -> A full group-algebra identity with controlled augmentation.
- **step-small-constant-terms** [claim](#stmt-prop-constant-term-small) / [proof](#pm-h-41): Compare an inverse differential primitive with Heegner logarithms, invert cleared factors after fixed truncation, and recover scalar square-root vanishing. -> Uniformly small constant coefficients with a diagonal choice of weights.
- **step-positive-index-unfolding** [claim](#stmt-lem-one-index-unfolding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-one-index-unfolding): Unfold the norm-one orbit and isolate its compact stabilizer without growing averaging denominators. -> A positive-index coefficient factored into Heisenberg and unary-period contributions.
- **step-simultaneous-unary-nonvanishing** [claim](#stmt-lem-simultaneous-unary-nonvanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-simultaneous-unary-nonvanishing): Check each root number separately, choose an inverse pair of finite-order twists outside exceptional sets, and apply the line-lift criterion. -> Nonzero unary periods at compatible positive cut weights.
- **step-uncontrolled-content** [claim](#stmt-lem-bounded-uncontrolled-content) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bounded-uncontrolled-content): Interpolate nonzero unary periods, avoid limiting moving-Euler zeros, and recover a coefficient from a fixed finite average. -> Bounded positive content compatible with small constant terms.
- **step-fixed-place-spanning** [claim](#stmt-lem-fixed-place-spanning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fixed-place-spanning): Filter local theta coinvariants by matrix rank and use a nonzero localized spanning minor. -> Finite replacement expressions with bounded denominators.
- **step-combined-theta-output** [claim](#stmt-prop-bounded-content) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bounded-content): Impose fixed-place controls by bounded spanning and retain a surviving positive coefficient. -> One theta family satisfying all integral, content, and local controls.
- **step-canonical-ordinary-level** [claim](#stmt-lem-canonical-ordinary-level) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-canonical-ordinary-level): Identify multiplicative flags by Cartier duality and extend branch idempotents through finite normalization. -> Canonical finite etale ordinary cusp charts without center-variable ramification.
- **step-dual-fock-functor** [claim](#stmt-lem-dual-fock-functor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dual-fock-functor): Define divided powers by integral duality and descend filtrations from local splittings. -> Integral coefficient operations compatible with base change and boundary filtrations.
- **step-cusp-base-change** [claim](#stmt-lem-cusp-base-change) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cusp-base-change): Lift positive coefficients using ample-line vanishing and transport them along free index orbits. -> Cusp sections modulo precision over the full tame Artin ring.
- **step-integral-ordinary-traces** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordinary-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordinary-trace): Compute coordinate-root traces and differential scaling on the divided-power lattice. -> Integral normalized operators and preserved comparison eigenrelations.
- **step-artin-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-lem-artin-content-cancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-artin-content-cancellation): Invert a polynomial with nonzero constant coefficient by a finite nilpotent geometric series. -> Uniform cancellation with bounded loss of precision.
- **step-classical-cusp-congruence** [claim](#stmt-prop-cusp-congruence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cusp-congruence): Extend by Hasse powers, lift by Serre vanishing, and cancel bounded positive content from every Hecke relation. -> Homomorphisms from the full classical operator order to the truncated tame ring.
- **step-weak-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-weak-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weak-transfer): Verify the individual holomorphic pseudocoefficient sign property and apply the simultaneous base-change input. -> A single conjugate-dual formal parameter with full infinite characters.
- **step-transfer-algebraicity** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer-algebraicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-algebraicity): Use regularity and unitary generic bounds to prove individual self-duality and construct parity-correcting character twists. -> Regular algebraic conjugate-self-dual twists of all cuspidal factors.
- **step-simultaneous-transfer** [claim](#stmt-prop-simultaneous-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-simultaneous-transfer): Retain the same weak parameter at all split places and use its nonsplit unramified factors. -> Simultaneous infinite and finite local transfer.
- **step-racsd-galois-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-racsd-galois-input) / [proof](#pm-h-56): Apply the stated established Galois representation input with its monodromy-domination compatibility. -> Polarized de Rham Galois factors and local compatibility.
- **step-galois-package** [claim](#stmt-prop-galois-package) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-galois-package): Attach Galois representations to algebraic factor twists and sum all shifted copies with the cohomological normalization. -> Four-dimensional cusp-system representations with exact weights and local bounds.
- **step-local-hodge-newton-contacts** [claim](#stmt-lem-two-contacts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-two-contacts): Use unit-root formulas, weak admissibility, and canonical slope subobjects. -> Local flags and uniformly convergent character quotient and subline.
- **step-binary-inertia** [claim](#stmt-lem-binary-inertia) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-binary-inertia): Separate Frobenius allocations with resultants and exclude actual binary monodromy within each shifted cuspidal copy. -> Actual inertia triviality on the binary primary projection.
- **step-steinberg-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-steinberg-control) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-steinberg-control): Use parahoric invariants to bound predicted Steinberg blocks and then actual monodromy. -> Unipotent inertia with the stated rank bound.
- **step-nilpotent-control** [claim](#stmt-prop-nilpotent-control) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nilpotent-control): Filter Jacquet allocations, isolate scalar-action grades on the separated locus, and annihilate the complementary filtration. -> Reduced-order congruence retaining every tame coefficient.
- **step-bounded-algebra-generators** [claim](#stmt-lem-bounded-algebra-generators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bounded-algebra-generators): Combine bounded Galois generation, polynomial identities, characteristic equations, and Nakayama. -> Uniformly many Galois-monomial module generators.
- **step-finite-value-admissibility** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-value-admissibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-value-admissibility): Choose finite quotient sections in the bounded generator presentation and approximate finitely many limiting matrix values. -> Admissible finite-stage representatives and uniform local identities.
- **step-corner-minors** [claim](#stmt-lem-corner-minors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-corner-minors): Decompose determinant monomials into corner paths and diagonal cycles and test in the faithful reduced ambient ring. -> Intrinsic determinant identities preserved under algebra maps.
- **step-cyclotomic-corner** [claim](#stmt-prop-cyclotomic-corner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cyclotomic-corner): Extract a cyclotomic extension quotient, impose strict local splitting by minors, and kill its global unit class by local logarithms. -> Factorization of the unwanted character corner through the elliptic block.
- **step-no-stable-last-line** [claim](#stmt-lem-no-stable-y-line) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-stable-y-line): Assume a vanished corner, recover an actual geometric character from finite matrix relations, and contradict its conjugate weight sum on fixed local units. -> Nonvanishing of the extension corner at every ambient field.
- **step-fitting-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-cor-fitting-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-fitting-vanishing): Use reduced faithful field testing to kill the annihilator and apply the adjugate containment for the zeroth Fitting ideal. -> Vanishing zeroth Fitting ideal.
- **step-extension-extraction** [claim](#stmt-prop-extension-extraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-extension-extraction): Map corner functionals to triangular extensions, impose strict local splitting over the full Artin ring, and compute lengths by elementary divisors. -> An injective Greenberg extension module of length at least the truncation.
- **step-main-length-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-65): Compare incompatible upper and lower lengths, exclude torsion of the Heegner trace, and apply the arithmetic reduction. -> The unrestricted low-corank converse and finiteness of the entire Tate–Shafarevich group.
- **step-sylvester-application** [claim](#stmt-cor-sylvester) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-sylvester): Retain both Tate–Shafarevich descent terms, bound the full Selmer corank, and use the negative root sign after applying the converse. -> Rank one, finite Tate–Shafarevich group, and rational cube-sum existence.
- **step-global-torus-factor** [claim](#stmt-lem-global-torus-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-global-torus-factor): Compare common Artin measure factors, identify dual components with character cohomology, and retain the degree-lattice correction. -> The normalized factor ratio and its degree-one splitting specialization.
- **step-nonstandard-characteristic-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-nonstandard-change-characteristic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nonstandard-change-characteristic): Construct common definable parameters and differential-form measures, clear discriminants and volumes, and transfer constructible vanishing. -> Uniform characteristic-zero nonstandard identities outside a finite residue-characteristic set.

Let $y\\in A_0(K)$ be the norm of the conductor-one Heegner point under a modular parametrization taking the cusp $\\infty$ to zero.           "anchor": "stmt-lem-base-green",
          "exact_quote": "If the compact Selmer line of $W/K$ has nonzero localization, then $H^1_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}}(K,W)=H^1_{\\overline{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}}}(K,W)=0$. The only remaining possibility is the following *strict case* in the $V$ summand: $r=1$, and the compact Selmer space is generated by a class $a$ such that $a^c=a$ and every localization of $a$ is zero. In that case $$\\label{eq:base-green-bases}\n H^1_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}}(K,V)=\\langle a,h\\rangle,\n \\qquad\n H^1_{\\overline{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}}}(K,V)=\\langle a,h^c\\rangle,$$ where $h$ has nonzero localization at $v$ and zero localization at $v^c$. The $V^{DD'}$ summand always has zero Greenberg space.",

        }

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tame-primes",
          "exact_quote": "There are rational primes $q_i\\notin S$, split completely in $L$, distinguished places $\\mathfrak q_i$ of $K$ above them, cyclic $p$-quotients $\\Delta_i$ of the ring class group of conductor $q_i$, and surjections $$\\ell_i:G_K\\longrightarrow\\Delta_i\\simeq\\mathbb Z/p^{n_i}\\mathbb Z,\n \\qquad n_i\\longrightarrow\\infty,$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *$\\ell_i$ is unramified outside the pair above $q_i$, its restriction to inertia at $\\mathfrak q_i$ is surjective, $\\ell_i^c=-\\ell_i$, and $\\ell_i|_{G_{K_s}}=0$ for every $s\\in S$.*\n\n2.  *$v_p(q_i-1)-n_i$ is bounded. At either place above $p$, the full ring class field of conductor $q_i$ is unramified and its local degree has bounded $p$-part.*\n\n3.  *Frobenius at $\\mathfrak q_i$, on all fixed data unramified outside $S$, tends to an element $\\gamma\\in G_L$ such that $$\\epsilon(\\gamma)=1,\\qquad\n     \\det(V(\\gamma)-1)\\ne0,\\qquad\n     \\mathcal C(\\gamma)\\text{ is not a root of unity}.$$ Moreover $\\gamma$ fixes all $p$-power radicals of a fixed, conjugation-stable finite list in $K^\\times$ spanning $\\mathcal O_{K,S}^{\\times}\\otimes\\mathbb Q_p$ under the Kummer map.",
          "source_id": "PM-002.2"
        }

Coefficient reduction and multiplication by $t$ give short exact sequences of admissible cochain complexes $$\\label{eq:coefficient-sequence}\n 0\\longrightarrow C^\\bullet(M_{b-1}^{\\eta})\n \\xrightarrow{\\ t\\ } C^\\bullet(M_b^{\\eta})\n \\longrightarrow C^\\bullet(V\\otimes k)\\longrightarrow0.$$ Zero local conditions at $p$ and local Kummer conditions are preserved by the resulting division of a class with zero specialization by $t$.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-bounded-content",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-theta-family",
        "step-small-constant-terms",
        "step-uncontrolled-content",
        "step-fixed-place-spanning"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-bounded-content",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the truncation $b_*$ selected in Section <a href=\"#sec:tame\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:tame\">3</a>. There exist positive integers $m_i\\to\\infty$, tending $p$-adically to zero on the prescribed torsion branch, and theta forms $\\Theta_i$ of weight $(m_i,m_i,1;-1)$ with the following simultaneous properties.*\n\n1.  *Their ordinary monomial-dual lattices have a uniform denominator bound, coefficientwise in the full tame group algebra and after every fixed truncated substitution used in the argument. One fixed scaling clears this bound.*\n\n2.  *All constant Fourier–Jacobi coefficients tend to zero through degree $b_*-1$ of the tame variable, in compatible ordinary differential bases, uniformly at the boundary representatives. This is a full-ring estimate in $R_{b_*}$.*\n\n3.  *At augmentation, a nonconstant Fourier–Jacobi coefficient has an integral-frame evaluation of valuation bounded above by a constant independent of $i$. Thus the forms do not acquire unbounded common content.*\n\n4.  *At both distinguished places above $p$, the fixed canonical $(2,1,1)$ level is $J$ and the operators $p^{-2}U_2,p^{-2}U_3$ have value $1$. At every controlled upper $(2,2)$ place, including $q_i$, the expanding center is invertible and the last binary spherical data are those of the final pair in <a href=\"#eq:theta-eigenparameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-eigenparameters\">[eq:theta-eigenparameters]</a>. At the specified unramified Steinberg places the level is $(3,1)$ parahoric, without an additional refinement. All nonsplit finite places are hyperspecial.*\n\n5.  *At split good spherical places the Hecke parameters are <a href=\"#eq:theta-eigenparameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta-eigenparameters\">[eq:theta-eigenparameters]</a> with $\\eta=\\mathcal C^{m_i}\\nu_i$.*\n\n*All constants may depend on the fixed original data, the fixed local tests and $b_*$, but not on $i,q_i,n_i$ or the growing weights.",
          "source_id": "PM-002.2"
        }
      ],

        "Bounded uncontrolled content",
        "Explicit binary operator allocation"
      ],
      "method": "Impose fixed-place controls by bounded spanning and retain a surviving positive coefficient.",
      "output": "One theta family satisfying all integral, content, and local controls.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-bounded-content"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-racsd-galois-input",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Pi$ be a regular algebraic conjugate-self-dual cuspidal representation of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_d(\\mathbb A_L)$ and fix $\\iota:\\overline{\\mathbb Q}_p\\simeq\\mathbb C$. There is a continuous semisimple representation $r_p(\\Pi)$ of $G_L$, with $$r_p(\\Pi)^c\\simeq r_p(\\Pi)^\\vee\\epsilon^{1-d}.$$ It is de Rham above $p$. If its first infinitesimal coordinates at an embedding are $b_1,\\ldots,b_d$, its de Rham weights there are $(d-1)/2-b_j$. At every finite place $w$, $$\\iota\\mathop{\\mathrm{WD}}\\bigl(r_p(\\Pi)|_{G_{L_w}}\\bigr)^{\\mathrm{F\\text{-}ss}}\n \\preceq\n \\operatorname{rec}_w\n     \\bigl(\\Pi_w\\otimes|\\det|_w^{(1-d)/2}\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:racsd-compatibility}$$ Here $\\preceq$ means equality after semisimplifying the Weil representation and domination of monodromy partitions on each inertial class.         }

          "exact_quote": "For every characteristic-zero cusp eigensystem in <a href=\"#prop:cusp-congruence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cusp-congruence\">33</a>, there is a continuous semisimple representation $$\\rho_\\pi:G_L\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_4(\\overline{\\mathbb Q}_p),\n \\qquad\n \\rho_\\pi^c\\simeq\\rho_\\pi^\\vee\\epsilon^{-3}.$$ It is unramified at every finite place nonsplit over $F$. At every split finite place $v=ww^c$, $$\\iota\\mathop{\\mathrm{WD}}\\bigl(\\rho_\\pi|_{G_{L_w}}\\bigr)^{\\mathrm{F\\text{-}ss}}\n \\preceq\n \\operatorname{rec}_w\n        \\bigl(\\pi_v\\otimes|\\det|_w^{-3/2}\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:four-dimensional-compatibility}$$ The representation is de Rham above $p$, and its increasing weights at either distinguished $w\\mid p$ are $$(h_1,h_2,h_3,h_4)\n  =(1-\\lambda'_1,\\,2-\\lambda'_2,\\,\n    3-\\lambda'_3,\\,-\\lambda'_4).\n \\label{eq:hodge-weights}$$ The gaps $h_3-h_2$ and $h_4-h_3$ tend to infinity with the choices in <a href=\"#eq:shifted-weight\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:shifted-weight\">[eq:shifted-weight]</a>.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-corner-minors",
          "exact_quote": "Choose four rows of a stack of $4\\times4$ matrices in $B$, with column indices $1,2,x,y$. If the row indices consist of the column indices with $y$ replaced by $u$, then its signed determinant is a well-defined element of $D_{uy}$. The same assertion holds for a square minor whenever canceling common row and column indices leaves precisely one surplus row and one surplus column; if no indices remain, its value lies in $T$. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-low-corank-converse**: For an elliptic curve over the rationals, full prime-power Selmer corank zero or one determines both the analytic rank and the Mordell–Weil rank and implies finiteness of the entire Tate–Shafarevich group.
- **invariant-same-module-length-contradiction**: Under the torsion assumption on the conductor-one Heegner trace, the cohomological upper bound and extracted extension lower bound concern the same admissible Greenberg module over the full truncated tame ring and are incompatible.
- **invariant-sylvester-prime-cases**: The stated prime congruence classes give cubic elliptic curves of analytic and Mordell–Weil rank one with finite Tate–Shafarevich group, and each such prime is a sum of two rational cubes.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-higher-corank**: Infer analytic rank and Tate–Shafarevich finiteness from an arbitrary full Selmer corank. -> The theorem's implication is stated only in the low-corank regime.
- **anti-other-base-fields**: Apply the unrestricted converse directly to an elliptic curve over an arbitrary number field. -> The auxiliary extensions in the proof do not enlarge the base-field scope of the main theorem.
- **anti-leading-coefficient**: Compute the refined Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer leading coefficient or the exact order of the Tate–Shafarevich group from this converse. -> Rank equality and group finiteness do not supply the refined leading-term identity.
- **anti-rank-only-input**: Replace the full Selmer corank hypothesis by knowledge of the Mordell–Weil rank alone. -> A divisible primary contribution from the Tate–Shafarevich group also contributes to full Selmer corank.
- **anti-characterwise-congruence**: Replace the full tame-ring congruence by separate evaluations at individual characters. -> Characterwise evaluation discards the nilpotent parameter used to compare module lengths.
- **anti-ordinary-source-assumption**: Require ordinary reduction of the original elliptic curve because the cusp-lifting argument uses an ordinary locus. -> This introduces a restriction absent from the theorem and confuses the target geometric ordinary locus with reduction of the source curve.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main converse and its cube-sum application establish arithmetic implications and existence rather than a finite procedure for computing full Selmer coranks or producing rational points. The proof explicitly requires an infinite limiting construction: “Fix a nonprincipal ultrafilter $\mathcal U$ on the prime indices.” The admissibility definition further requires that “the limiting identity holds on *every* sequence of arguments.” These requirements occur at the tame-deformation setup and the admissible-cochain definition. Finite polynomial calculations could check isolated algebraic sublemmas, but would not decide the stated arithmetic invariants or certify the global converse.

    "reason": "The main converse and its cube-sum application establish arithmetic implications and existence rather than a finite procedure for computing full Selmer coranks or producing rational points. The proof explicitly requires an infinite limiting construction: “Fix a nonprincipal ultrafilter $\\mathcal U$ on the prime indices.” The admissibility definition further requires that “the limiting identity holds on *every* sequence of arguments.” These requirements occur at the tame-deformation setup and the admissible-cochain definition. Finite polynomial calculations could check isolated algebraic sublemmas, but would not decide the stated arithmetic invariants or certify the global converse.",
    "reference_code": null,
    "test_vectors": []
  },
  "pm_id": "PM-002.2",
  "schema": "pm.triptych.v2"
}

| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite truncated-ring inverse supplies a concrete arithmetic routine for cancelling a polynomial factor with controlled precision loss. This implements an auxiliary algebraic step; the Selmer converse does not supply a procedure for computing its arithmetic hypothesis. |

Such a check addresses the finite algebraic calculation while leaving the global arithmetic proof obligations separate. |

The paper states no running-time or storage bound for this construction. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite corner modules and matrix units organize the limiting extension argument, but the paper gives no executable representation or maintenance operations for those modules over the henselian ultraproduct coefficient ring. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Uniformly bounded algebra generators control admissibility of limiting Galois coefficients. That bound supplies neither a hardware mapping nor an arithmetic throughput or memory contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The elliptic-curve theorem relates full Selmer corank to ranks and finiteness of the Tate–Shafarevich group over the rationals. It establishes no finite-field security assumption, attack construction, or cryptographic hardness result. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The bounded measure represents normalized unary theta jets on integral position lattices. It does not define a learned model, training objective, or statistical generalization guarantee. |

It gives no optimization objective, finite search bound, or operations-research solver construction. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Admissible cochains require uniformly bounded finite-stage representatives and identities on every sequence of arguments. These are mathematical limit conditions rather than a message-passing, replication, or consensus protocol. |
| other (Exact commutative algebra) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The finite elementary-divisor decomposition over the principal Artin ring constructs the dual of each cyclic summand as an ideal. With a decomposition supplied, this gives explicit dual summands and their lengths; it does not compute the global limiting corner module. |

Finite-stage cochain identities are required modulo precisions $A_i\\longrightarrow\\infty$ along $\\mathcal U$, with $A_i\\le n_i-c_b$. Equivalently, the limiting identity holds on *every* sequence of arguments. Cohomology is the cohomology of this cochain complex, so coboundaries are also required to be admissible.           "anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-extension-extraction",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-prop-extension-extraction</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Put $M=D_{fy}\\otimes_{T,\\lambda}R_b$. A functional $M\\to R_b$ gives a triangular extension of $y$ by $f$ while retaining the separate $x$ block. We check the only additional product relation. The path from $y$ through $x$ to $f$ has image in $$D_{fx}D_{xy}=D_{fx}D_{xf}D_{fy}$$ by <a href=\"#eq:xy-factorization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:xy-factorization\">[eq:xy-factorization]</a>. Its first two factors form a crossing diagonal cycle, killed by <a href=\"#eq:crossing-cycles\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:crossing-cycles\">[eq:crossing-cycles]</a> after applying $\\lambda$. All other discarded products are killed by the same cycle relation or by the diagonal representation. Hence the triangular assignment is an algebra homomorphism out of $B$. As in <a href=\"#eq:xy-cohomology\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:xy-cohomology\">[eq:xy-cohomology]</a>, a split extension must have its splitting preserved by $B$ and then by its idempotents; it forces the functional to be zero. This proves injectivity into extension cohomology. Moreover $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(y,f)=V\\otimes\\psi^{\\pm1},$$ and <a href=\"#lem:finite-value-admissibility\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:finite-value-admissibility\">44</a> proves admissibility with coefficients in the full ring $R_b$.\n\nAt a conjugate $p$-place, use the same common-kernel stack $S_g=\\rho_*(g)-Y_{\\bar w}(g)$. Choose four rows of types $f,f,x,f$, with either of the two $f$ positions as the final row. By <a href=\"#lem:corner-minors\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:corner-minors\">45</a> the vanishing four-row determinants are identities in the corresponding entry of $D_{fy}$, and they survive in the triangular extension. The first two $f$ rows and one $x$ row give a $3\\times3$ minor on the competing columns whose residue is invertible by $H^0(L_{\\bar w},V)=0$ and $x\\ne y$. It is therefore a unit over $R_b$. Solve these equations with $y$ coordinate one. Varying the final $f$ row proves that every other $f$ equation is satisfied. The $x$ coordinate is zero and the $y$ rows vanish. This produces a common $y$-eigenline and splits the local extension over the full Artin ring, including its nilpotent coefficients.\n\nThe Greenberg condition is full at $\\Sigma_p$, so no splitting is required there; it is relaxed at the remaining fixed bad places. Outside those places and the moving places the representations are unramified, as required for admissible classes. Thus the preceding local splitting is precisely the one-sided condition of <a href=\"#prop:green-length\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:green-length\">12</a>, and gives <a href=\"#eq:green-injection\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:green-injection\">[eq:green-injection]</a>.\n\nFitting ideals commute with base change for finite modules, so <a href=\"#eq:fitting-vanishing\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:fitting-vanishing\">[eq:fitting-vanishing]</a> gives $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Fitt}}_{0,R_b}(M)=0$. The finite module $M$ over the principal Artin ring $R_b$ has an elementary-divisor decomposition $$M\\simeq\\bigoplus_j R_b/(t^{a_j}),\n                   \\qquad 1\\leq a_j\\leq b.$$ Its zeroth Fitting ideal is $(t^{\\sum_j a_j})$, with the evident interpretation for free summands $a_j=b$. Vanishing is equivalent to $\\sum_j a_j\\geq b$. Each summand and its $R_b$-dual have length $a_j$, since $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{R_b}(R_b/(t^{a_j}),R_b)=(t^{b-a_j})$. Thus the source in <a href=\"#eq:green-injection\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:green-injection\">[eq:green-injection]</a> has length at least $b$. ◻",
          "source_object": "proof-stmt-prop-extension-extraction"
        },

          "exact_quote": "One has $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Fitt}}_{0,T}(D_{fy})=0.\n \\label{eq:fitting-vanishing}$$",
          "source_object": "stmt-cor-fitting-vanishing"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-low-corank-rational-elliptic-scope** (hypothesis): The main converse requires an elliptic curve over the rationals and independently established full prime-power Selmer corank zero or one. Blocked use: ['Apply the conclusion to higher Selmer coranks.', 'Extend the conclusion to arbitrary number fields or higher-dimensional abelian varieties.', 'Treat a finite-level Selmer dimension as the full prime-power corank without further justification.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Selmer converse in coranks zero and one for every elliptic curve over ℚ and every prime <i>p</i>: if the full <i>p</i>-power Selmer group has ℤ<sub><i>p</i></sub>-corank $`r\in\{0,1\}`$, then the analytic and Mordell–Weil ranks both equal <i>r</i>, and the entire Tate–Shafarevich group is finite. As an application at the additive prime 3, we prove that for every prime $`\ell\equiv4,7,8\pmod9`$, the cubic $`X^3+Y^3=\ell Z^3`$ has analytic and Mordell–Weil rank one and finite Tate–Shafarevich group. In particular, every such <i>ℓ</i> is a sum of two rational cubes.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $A_0/\mathbb Q$ be an elliptic curve and let $p$ be a rational prime. Write $\operatorname{Sha}(A_0/\mathbb Q)$ for the kernel of localization from the Weil–Châtelet group to the product of the local Weil–Châtelet groups. The full $p$-power Selmer group, with local Kummer conditions at every place, fits into the exact sequence $$0\longrightarrow A_0(\mathbb Q)\otimes\mathbb Q_p/\mathbb Z_p
 \longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Sel}}_{p^\infty}(A_0/\mathbb Q)
 \longrightarrow\operatorname{Sha}(A_0/\mathbb Q)[p^\infty]
 \longrightarrow0.$$ Thus its corank measures both the Mordell–Weil rank and any divisible $p$-primary contribution from $\operatorname{Sha}$. Put $$s_p(A_0)=\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb Z_p}\mathop{\mathrm{Sel}}_{p^\infty}(A_0/\mathbb Q),
 \qquad a(A_0)=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(A_0,s).$$ The Selmer converse asks whether this arithmetic invariant at a specified prime determines the analytic order of vanishing. We prove the converse in coranks zero and one without restrictions on the curve or the prime.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (The unrestricted low-corank Selmer converse). *For every elliptic curve $A_0/\mathbb Q$, every prime $p$, and $r\in\{0,1\}$, $$s_p(A_0)=r
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 a(A_0)=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_{\mathbb Z}A_0(\mathbb Q)=r,
 \qquad \#\operatorname{Sha}(A_0/\mathbb Q)<\infty.$$ There is no restriction on reduction type, complex multiplication, the residual representation, rational torsion, or rational isogenies.*

</div>

The finiteness conclusion concerns the entire Tate–Shafarevich group, not just its $p$-primary part. In particular, the Kummer sequence then shows that all prime-power Selmer coranks agree with the rank determined at $p$. The theorem gives rank equality and finiteness; it does not evaluate the leading coefficient in the refined Birch–Swinnerton-Dyer formula.

A concrete application comes from Sylvester’s cube-sum problem: which rational primes are sums of two rational cubes? Section <a href="#sec:sylvester" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sylvester">10</a> applies the theorem at $p=3$ to show that, for every prime $\ell\equiv4,7,8\pmod9$, the cubic $X^3+Y^3=\ell Z^3$ has analytic and Mordell–Weil rank one and finite $\operatorname{Sha}$. These cases have also been treated by direct Heegner-point methods, including recent work of Yin and Burungale–Tian (Yin 2026a, 2026b; A. Burungale and Tian 2026) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Yin2026Sylvester47](#ref-Yin2026Sylvester47), [Yin2026SylvesterGZ](#ref-Yin2026SylvesterGZ), [BurungaleTian2026Sylvester](#ref-BurungaleTian2026Sylvester)]. The deduction here illustrates the use of an unrestricted converse at a prime of additive reduction: Satgé’s paired isogeny descents, with their Tate–Shafarevich terms retained, bound the full $3$-power Selmer corank by one, and the negative root sign selects analytic rank one.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The converse problem and its development

The conjecture of Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer grew from computations relating rational points to the behavior of the elliptic $L$-function near its central point (Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer 1965) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[BirchSwinnertonDyer1965](#ref-BirchSwinnertonDyer1965)]. Coates and Wiles proved an early analytic-to-arithmetic implication for elliptic curves with complex multiplication (Coates and Wiles 1977) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[CoatesWiles1977](#ref-CoatesWiles1977)]. Modularity supplies analytic continuation and the functional equation for every elliptic curve over $\mathbb Q$ (Breuil et al. 2001) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BCDT2001](#ref-BCDT2001)]. The height formula of Gross and Zagier and Kolyvagin’s Euler systems give rank equality and finiteness of $\operatorname{Sha}$ when the analytic rank is at most one (Gross and Zagier 1986; Kolyvagin 1990) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GrossZagier1986](#ref-GrossZagier1986), [Kolyvagin1990](#ref-Kolyvagin1990)]. Kato’s zeta elements provide another fundamental route from a nonzero central value to arithmetic finiteness (Kato 2004) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Kato2004](#ref-Kato2004)]. The converse reverses this direction: a small Selmer group must force a nonzero central value or derivative. More generally, the $p$-converse predicts $a(A_0)=s_p(A_0)$; see Keller and Yin (2024b, Conjecture A) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[KellerYin2024](#ref-KellerYin2024)].

In rank zero, Skinner–Urban’s main-conjecture theorem gives the Iwasawa divisibility opposite to the one obtained from Kato’s Euler system. It yields the converse at odd good ordinary primes under residual irreducibility and an auxiliary ramification hypothesis (Skinner and Urban 2014) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[SkinnerUrban2014](#ref-SkinnerUrban2014)]. In rank one, the problem becomes the non-torsion of a Heegner point. Skinner uses anticyclotomic Iwasawa theory to deduce this from a one-dimensional Selmer group with nonzero localization at $p$, under ordinary, residual, and ramification hypotheses (Skinner 2020, Theorem B) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Skinner2020](#ref-Skinner2020)]. Wei Zhang proves nonvanishing of derived Heegner classes by level-raising and rank-lowering arguments. Under his ordinary and residual hypotheses, this proves the corank-one converse without the nonzero-localization assumption (Zhang 2014, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Zhang2014](#ref-Zhang2014)]. These two approaches have different hypotheses; their common task is to recover analytic nonvanishing from arithmetic information that does not itself exhibit a non-torsion Heegner point.

Later work has removed many of the restrictions in these arguments. For good ordinary $p>3$ with irreducible $A_0[p]$, Burungale–Castella–Skinner remove the earlier auxiliary residual-ramification conditions (A. Burungale, Castella, and Skinner 2025) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[BCS2025](#ref-BCS2025)]. Reducible residual representations require a different analysis: Castella–Grossi–Lee–Skinner prove the converse at odd good Eisenstein primes with a local-character restriction, and Keller–Yin remove that restriction and treat multiplicative and potentially good ordinary cases, including rational $p$-torsion and some additive reduction (Castella et al. 2022; Keller and Yin 2024a, 2024b) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[CGLS2022](#ref-CGLS2022), [KellerYinSemistable2024](#ref-KellerYinSemistable2024), [KellerYin2024](#ref-KellerYin2024)]. At good supersingular primes greater than three, Castella–Wan prove the corank-one converse for semistable curves (Castella and Wan 2024) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[CastellaWan2024](#ref-CastellaWan2024)]. Castella treats multiplicative reduction under local and residual hypotheses (Castella 2024, Theorems 1.1 and 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Castella2024](#ref-Castella2024)]; the zeta-element constructions of Burungale–Skinner–Tian–Wan yield further supersingular rank-zero and ordinary applications (A. A. Burungale et al. 2024, Theorems 1.6 and 1.10) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[BSTW2024](#ref-BSTW2024)].

Complex multiplication also permits small-prime results. Burungale–Castella–Skinner–Tian prove the corank-one converse at good ordinary primes, including $2$ and $3$, when the associated Hecke character has conductor exactly divisible by the different of the CM field (A. Burungale et al. 2022, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[BCST2022](#ref-BCST2022)]. Burungale–Tian prove the rank-zero converse for every CM elliptic curve at every prime (A. A. Burungale and Tian 2026) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BurungaleTian2026](#ref-BurungaleTian2026)]. Kriz’s preprint states a low-corank converse at the ramified prime for curves with CM by the full ring of integers, together with its cube-sum application (Kriz 2022, Theorems 1.1 and 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Kriz2022](#ref-Kriz2022)].

The present proof uses two distinct results of OpenAI (2026) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[OpenAIGoldfeldDensity2026](#ref-OpenAIGoldfeldDensity2026)]: the unrestricted converse at $2$ settles that prime, while the analytic twist-density theorem supplies the auxiliary fields for the odd-prime argument. Their precise statements are recorded in Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">2</a>. The remaining construction keeps the specified odd prime arbitrary. In particular, it must control local conditions even when the elliptic curve is not ordinary and the residual representation is reducible.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof strategy: two incompatible lengths

Fix an odd prime $p$ and suppose $s_p(A_0)\in\{0,1\}$. The first goal is to produce a non-torsion Heegner point. The density input selects an imaginary quadratic field $K$ and a biquadratic CM field $L$ containing $K$. The rational Selmer spaces of $A_0$ and an auxiliary quadratic twist over $K$ are both lines; the latter is generated by a known non-torsion point. Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">2</a> shows that non-torsion of the conductor-one Heegner trace $y$ implies the desired conclusion by Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin. Assume, for a contradiction, that $y$ is torsion. We will obtain more Galois extensions than the Selmer hypothesis allows.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### The cohomological upper bound.

Let $V=T_pA_0\otimes_{\mathbb Z_p}\mathbb Q_p$. At a sequence of split auxiliary primes $q_i$, cyclic $p$-quotients of growing ring class groups give a tame character $\psi$ with coefficients in $$R_b=k[t]/(t^b),\qquad b\ge1,$$ where $k/\mathbb Q_p$ is finite and $\psi\equiv1\pmod t$. We form $\psi$ by taking the $p$-adic ultralimit of the finite-stage characters on sequences of Galois elements over $K$, and restrict it to $L$ below. Its cohomology is represented by bounded finite-stage cochains whose identities hold to uniformly increasing precision; Section <a href="#sec:tame" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tame">3</a> calls these cochains *admissible*. Choose a place $v$ of $K$ above $p$ and the places of $L$ above $v$. The one-sided Greenberg group $\mathcal H_b=H^1_{\mathrm{Gr}}(L,V\otimes\psi)$ allows unrestricted local classes at the chosen places and requires zero classes at their conjugates, with relaxed conditions at the remaining fixed places.

If the Selmer line of $A_0/K$ has nonzero localization at $p$, then $\mathcal H_b=0$. Otherwise that line is everywhere locally zero; we call this the strict case. There a Heisenberg commutator modifies the tame direction so that no nonzero base Greenberg class lifts to first order. The exact coefficient sequence then confines every admissible class to the last $t$-layer and gives $\mathop{\mathrm{length}}_{R_b}\mathcal H_b\le2$, independently of $b$. Consequently, with $$b_*=1\quad\text{outside the strict case},\qquad
 b_*=3\quad\text{in the strict case},$$ the cohomological upper bound is strictly smaller than $b_*$.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### A theta congruence retaining the tame parameter.

The weighted Heegner logarithm is a truncated $t$-series formed from unnormalized sums of local logarithms of ring-class Heegner points, weighted by the finite-stage tame characters. The torsion assumption on $y$ makes its constant coefficient tend to zero. In the strict case, conjugation and the zero localizations give the same conclusion for the next two coefficients. This logarithmic vanishing is distinct from the first-order obstruction used for the upper bound. It is the input to the automorphic construction.

Sections <a href="#sec:theta" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:theta">4</a>–<a href="#sec:content" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:content">6</a> construct a holomorphic theta family on a unitary group of signature $(3,1)$ at both real places of the maximal real subfield of $L$. The construction uses Weil’s oscillator representation and the theta and period framework of Kudla and Rallis (Weil 1964; Kudla 1994; Rallis 1984) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Weil1964](#ref-Weil1964), [Kudla1994](#ref-Kudla1994), [Rallis1984Injectivity](#ref-Rallis1984Injectivity)]. An algebraic square identity relates its constant term to toric periods, one of which specializes to the Heegner logarithm. The passage from raised CM values through an inverse differential operator to that logarithm follows the method of Bertolini–Darmon–Prasanna (Bertolini, Darmon, and Prasanna 2013) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[BertoliniDarmonPrasanna2013](#ref-BertoliniDarmonPrasanna2013)]; the argument here includes the additional bad-reduction comparisons. Uniform integral moment estimates make the constant terms small, while a separate positive Fourier–Jacobi coefficient has bounded divisibility and prevents the entire theta series from disappearing.

The geometric input to cusp lifting is the ordinary locus of the target PEL variety, not ordinary reduction of $A_0$. Using the compactification and ordinary deformation frameworks of Lan and Katz (Lan 2013, 2018; N. M. Katz 1981) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Lan2013](#ref-Lan2013), [Lan2018Ordinary](#ref-Lan2018Ordinary), [Katz1981SerreTate](#ref-Katz1981SerreTate)], Section <a href="#sec:cusp-lifting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cusp-lifting">7</a> lifts the theta series to genuine cusp forms modulo increasing powers of $p$. Its integral coefficient lattice and normalized Hecke traces preserve the whole truncated tame ring. Evaluating the congruence only at individual characters would discard the nilpotent parameter that measures the required length.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### The extension lower bound.

The passage from an automorphic congruence to a Galois extension goes back to Ribet and Wiles (Ribet 1976; Wiles 1990) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Ribet1976](#ref-Ribet1976), [Wiles1990](#ref-Wiles1990)]. Skinner–Urban use congruences on larger unitary groups (Skinner and Urban 2014) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[SkinnerUrban2014](#ref-SkinnerUrban2014)]; the semiordinary $\mathrm{GU}(3,1)$ construction of Castella–Liu–Wan is a close antecedent allowing a nonordinary two-dimensional source (Castella, Liu, and Wan 2022, n.d.) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[CastellaLiuWan2022](#ref-CastellaLiuWan2022), [CastellaLiuWanAddendum](#ref-CastellaLiuWanAddendum)]. Here Section <a href="#sec:parameters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:parameters">8</a> constructs four-dimensional characteristic-zero Galois representations from the cusp forms and proves the local identities needed to extract one-sided Greenberg classes. Two normalized parabolic operators are units, although the elliptic representation remains unrestricted at $p$.

The limiting trace separates into a two-dimensional twist of $V$ and two characters; this is not a decomposition assumed for each cuspidal Galois representation. Section <a href="#sec:extensions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:extensions">9</a> uses the off-diagonal modules of a finite limiting matrix algebra to record extensions between these constituents, in the spirit of the generalized matrix algebras of Bellaïche–Chenevier (Bellaïche and Chenevier 2009) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[BellaicheChenevier2009](#ref-BellaicheChenevier2009)]. The main tasks are to eliminate an unwanted cyclotomic extension quotient between the two characters and to impose the strict local condition on extensions of one character by the two-dimensional constituent. Uniformly many algebra generators reduce each limiting functional to finitely many matrix values, so the resulting cochains remain admissible. The local identities hold over the entire Artin ring, and a Fitting ideal calculation gives $$\mathop{\mathrm{length}}_{R_{b_*}}\mathcal H_{b_*}\ge b_*.$$ This contradicts the upper bound and proves that $y$ is non-torsion. Figure <a href="#fig:proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof">1</a> summarizes the comparison.

<figure id="fig:proof">

<figcaption>The two bounds concern the same admissible Greenberg module over $R_{b*}$. Nonzero localization gives upper bound $0$ and $b_{*} = 1$; the strict case gives upper bound at most $2$ and $b_{*} = 3$.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-proof"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:proof`)

The proof therefore separates into the arithmetic reduction and upper bound in Sections <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">2</a>–<a href="#sec:tame" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tame">3</a>, the theta and cusp congruence in Sections <a href="#sec:theta" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:theta">4</a>–<a href="#sec:cusp-lifting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cusp-lifting">7</a>, and the Galois construction and extraction in Sections <a href="#sec:parameters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:parameters">8</a>–<a href="#sec:extensions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:extensions">9</a>. Section <a href="#sec:sylvester" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sylvester">10</a> gives the cube-sum application. The simultaneous transfer in Section <a href="#subsec:simultaneous-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:simultaneous-transfer">8.1</a> uses Labesse’s base-change theorem, with its sign property verified for the holomorphic discrete series at both real places. Appendix <a href="#sec:nonstandard-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:nonstandard-transfer">11</a> records a separate normalized global measure comparison and a change-of-characteristic principle for nonstandard weighted orbital integrals.

<a id="sec:reduction"></a>
### Arithmetic reduction and conventions

The converse at $2$ settles the even prime. For an odd prime, we choose auxiliary quadratic fields so that the problem reduces to proving that one conductor-one Heegner trace is non-torsion. The resulting compact Selmer spaces will also supply the initial data for the tame deformation.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Selmer groups and established inputs

For a number field $M$, put $T_pA_0=\varprojlim_n A_0[p^n]$ and $V_pA_0=T_pA_0\otimes_{\mathbb Z_p}\mathbb Q_p$. For a finite place $v$ of $M$, the finite local condition is the Kummer image $$H^1_f(M_v,V_pA_0)
 =\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl(A_0(M_v)^{\wedge}_p\otimes_{\mathbb Z_p}\mathbb Q_p
       \longrightarrow H^1(M_v,V_pA_0)\bigr).$$ Here $A_0(M_v)^{\wedge}_p=\varprojlim_n A_0(M_v)/p^nA_0(M_v)$. The rational compact Selmer group is $$H^1_f(M,V_pA_0)=
 \ker\left(H^1(M,V_pA_0)\longrightarrow
   \prod_v H^1(M_v,V_pA_0)/H^1_f(M_v,V_pA_0)\right).$$ Continuous cohomology and the usual archimedean local conditions are understood. The Kummer exact sequences and local/global duality give <a id="eq:compact-corank"></a>$$\label{eq:compact-corank}
 \dim_{\mathbb Q_p}H^1_f(M,V_pA_0)
 =\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb Z_p}\mathop{\mathrm{Sel}}_{p^\infty}(A_0/M).$$ We use rational coefficients throughout the cohomological argument; integral lattices will be retained whenever a uniform valuation bound is required.

We use the following two established results.

<a id="stmt-thm-two-converse-input"></a>
<div id="thm:two-converse-input" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Established converse at $2$). *If $E/\mathbb Q$ is any elliptic curve and $\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb Z_2}\mathop{\mathrm{Sel}}_{2^\infty}(E/\mathbb Q)=r\in\{0,1\}$, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=1}L(E,s)=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}E(\mathbb Q)=r,
 \qquad \operatorname{Sha}(E/\mathbb Q)\text{ is finite}.$$*

</div>

<a id="stmt-thm-twist-density-input"></a>
<div id="thm:twist-density-input" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (Established analytic twist-density theorem). *For any elliptic curve $E/\mathbb Q$, among nonzero squarefree integers $d$ ordered by $|d|$, the quadratic twists $E^{(d)}$ of analytic rank zero and one each have density $1/2$. In particular the exceptional set of twists of analytic rank at least two has density zero.*

</div>

These are OpenAI (2026, Theorems 1.1 and 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[OpenAIGoldfeldDensity2026](#ref-OpenAIGoldfeldDensity2026)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:two-converse-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:two-converse-input">2</a> treats the prime $2$. For auxiliary-field selection we use the density-zero exceptional-set consequence of Theorem <a href="#thm:twist-density-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:twist-density-input">3</a> in fixed positive-density congruence classes. The remainder of the paper supplies the argument at odd primes.

Fix $A_0$ and $r=s_p(A_0)\in\{0,1\}$. By Theorem <a href="#thm:two-converse-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:two-converse-input">2</a>, we may assume $p>2$. Write $N$ for the conductor and $V=V_pA_0$. The Weil pairing gives $V\simeq V^*(1)$ with alternating pairing. The $p$-parity theorem (Dokchitser and Dokchitser 2010, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[DokchitserDokchitser2010](#ref-DokchitserDokchitser2010)] gives the global root number <a id="eq:root-sign"></a>$$\label{eq:root-sign}
 w(A_0)=(-1)^r.$$ Modularity and the Gross–Zagier–Kolyvagin theorems will be used in their rank-zero and rank-one forms (Breuil et al. 2001; Gross and Zagier 1986; Kolyvagin 1990) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[BCDT2001](#ref-BCDT2001), [GrossZagier1986](#ref-GrossZagier1986), [Kolyvagin1990](#ref-Kolyvagin1990)].

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Two auxiliary discriminants

<a id="stmt-lem-auxiliary-fields"></a>
<div id="lem:auxiliary-fields" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4**. *There are coprime negative odd fundamental discriminants $D,D'$ such that every prime dividing $2Np$ splits in both quadratic fields, $$\left(\frac{D}{|D'|}\right)=1,$$ and each of $A_0^D,A_0^{D'},A_0^{DD'}$ has the smallest analytic rank permitted by its sign. They may also be chosen so that $$K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt D),\qquad F=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{DD'}),\qquad
 L=\mathbb Q(\sqrt D,\sqrt{D'})$$ satisfy the following properties: $L/F$ is CM and unramified at every finite prime, $L$ has only the roots of unity $\{\pm1\}$, and $L$ does not contain the CM field of $A_0$ if $A_0$ has CM.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-auxiliary-fields"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-auxiliary-fields</summary>

*Proof.* Negative odd fundamental discriminants in any admissible fixed congruence class have positive density. The splitting conditions at $2Np$ are finitely many such conditions. We can exclude the finitely many unwanted quadratic fields and then apply Theorem <a href="#thm:twist-density-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:twist-density-input">3</a> to choose $D$ for which $A_0^D$ has analytic rank at most one. Its rank is then minimal for its sign.

Keep $D$ fixed. The conditions on $D'$ of being coprime to $D$, splitting $2Np$, and satisfying the displayed Jacobi-symbol condition again contain admissible congruence classes of positive density. For example, impose $D'\equiv-1\pmod{|D|}$ and $D'\equiv1$ at a sufficiently divisible modulus supported on $2Np$. The moduli are coprime, and $|D'|\equiv1\pmod{|D|}$ gives the required Jacobi symbol. Exclude the finitely many remaining forbidden fields. Apply the density theorem to both $A_0$ and $A_0^D$. The union of their two exceptional sets has density zero, so some $D'$ in the prescribed classes makes both $A_0^{D'}$ and $(A_0^D)^{D'}=A_0^{DD'}$ have analytic rank at most one.

This use of density is unchanged if parameters are described by fundamental discriminants: for squarefree $d$ the corresponding discriminant is $d$ or $4d$, so a zero-density exceptional set remains zero-density. In the odd classes used here the parameter itself is the discriminant.

The three quadratic subfields have discriminants $D,D',DD'$. Consequently the biquadratic discriminant formula gives $$|\operatorname{disc}(L)|=D^2(D')^2
   =\operatorname{disc}(F)^2.$$ The discriminant formula for $L/F$ therefore has relative discriminant of norm one, proving finite unramifiedness. The two imaginary subfields give the CM involution over the real field $F$. The exclusions made above remove any extra roots of unity and the possible CM field of $A_0$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-arithmetic-reduction"></a>
<div id="prop:arithmetic-reduction" class="proposition">

**Proposition 5**. *With these choices, $$\dim H^1_f(K,V)=1,
 \qquad
 \dim H^1_f(K,V^{DD'})=1.$$ The second line localizes nontrivially at both places of $K$ over $p$. Let $y\in A_0(K)$ be the norm of the conductor-one Heegner point under a modular parametrization taking the cusp $\infty$ to zero. If $y$ is non-torsion, Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> follows.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-arithmetic-reduction"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-arithmetic-reduction</summary>

*Proof.* For a fundamental discriminant prime to $N$, the quadratic-twist root-number formula is $w(A_0^d)=w(A_0)\chi_d(-N)$. The splitting conditions and the signs of $D,D'$ therefore give $$w(A_0^D)=w(A_0^{D'})=-(-1)^r,
 \qquad w(A_0^{DD'})=(-1)^r.$$ By Lemma <a href="#lem:auxiliary-fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:auxiliary-fields">4</a>, their analytic ranks are $1-r,1-r,r$, respectively. Gross–Zagier–Kolyvagin gives the same Mordell–Weil and $p$-power Selmer ranks and finite Sha for these three twists. Restriction and corestriction for the quadratic extension $K/\mathbb Q$ give $$H^1_f(K,V)
  =H^1_f(\mathbb Q,V)\oplus H^1_f(\mathbb Q,V^D),$$ and the analogous decomposition for $V^{DD'}$. Thus the two dimensions are $r+(1-r)=1$ and $r+(1-r)=1$.

For the second representation both summands come from twists with known analytic rank at most one. Its Selmer line is therefore spanned by a non-torsion global point on the corresponding twist over $K$. Such a point has nonzero image under the local logarithm at either place above $p$. Indeed, on a fixed $p$-adic completion, a finite-index open subgroup is a formal group on which the logarithm is injective after shrinking; a point with zero logarithm consequently has a torsion multiple in that subgroup, and is torsion. This argument uses no reduction hypothesis.

Every prime dividing $N$ splits in $K$, so the classical Heegner hypothesis holds. The Gross–Zagier formula identifies the nonzero height of $y$ with simple vanishing of $$L(A_0/K,s)=L(A_0,s)L(A_0^D,s)$$ at $s=1$. Since the second factor has order $1-r$, a non-torsion $y$ implies $a(A_0)=r$. The analytic rank-zero/one theorems then give $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}A_0(\mathbb Q)=r$ and finiteness of the whole $\operatorname{Sha}(A_0/\mathbb Q)$. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:torsion-assumption"></a>
For the rest of the proof we assume $$\label{eq:torsion-assumption}
 y=0\quad\text{in }A_0(K)\otimes\mathbb Q.$$ We will derive a contradiction. In particular, every subsequent choice is made after $A_0,p,D,D'$ have been fixed. The first Selmer line in Proposition <a href="#prop:arithmetic-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:arithmetic-reduction">5</a> may have zero localization, whereas the second cannot. Section <a href="#sec:tame" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tame">3</a> distinguishes these two possibilities before deforming the representations.

<a id="sec:conventions"></a>
#### Coefficient, character, and measure conventions

Fix embeddings into $\mathbb C$ and $\overline{\mathbb Q}_p$. Let $\Sigma$ be the CM type on $L$ induced from one embedding of $K$, and let $\Sigma_p$ be the corresponding half of the places of $L$ above $p$. Complex conjugation is denoted by $c$. All the fields introduced above are split at $p$, and the places of $K$ above $p$ will be distinguished according to this choice.

Smooth characters and normalized Satake parameters are evaluated on uniformizers using the reciprocity convention corresponding to geometric Frobenius. Write $\epsilon$ for the cyclotomic character. A de Rham weight of $\epsilon^{-1}$ is called $1$. When a local cohomology computation instead uses arithmetic Frobenius, this will be stated; inversion changes neither the vanishing assertions nor the invertibility requirements used below.

Characters of $\mathop{\mathrm{U}}(1)_{L/F}=\ker\mathop{\mathrm{N}}_{L/F}$ are pulled back to characters of $L^\times\backslash\mathbb A_L^\times$ by $z\mapsto z/z^c$; the same convention applies to $K/\mathbb Q$. Choose a character $\mathcal C$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{U}}(1)_{K/\mathbb Q}$ with infinity type $s\mapsto s$ in the chosen embedding and with finite conductor supported on split primes away from $pDD'N$. These primes may be required to split completely in $L$. The ray-class construction gives such a character: a sufficiently deep split conductor removes the finite unit obstruction, and the prescribed algebraic character on principal ideles then extends across the finite ray-class quotient. We use the same symbol for its norm pullback to $\mathop{\mathrm{U}}(1)_{L/F}$ and for the corresponding algebraic $p$-adic character.

At the distinguished split component above $p$, its finite smooth part has uniformizer value of valuation $-1$ with these conventions. This records the algebraic infinity contribution; it is not a claim that the smooth character is unitary in the $p$-adic norm. All archimedean weights $m\to+\infty$ below also satisfy $m\to0$ $p$-adically on a fixed residue branch, including the finite torsion orders of the characters raised to the $m$th power.

Let $k$ be a sufficiently large fixed finite extension of $\mathbb Q_p$, with ring of integers $\mathcal O$. We enlarge it finitely when fixed algebraic normalization factors require this. Valuation bounds refer to fixed bases and integral lattices. A *uniform* bound may depend on the fixed data and on a fixed truncation degree, but not on the moving prime, its growing $p$-power quotient, or the growing weights. When coefficient fields for classical eigensystems vary, we retain their embeddings in $\overline{\mathbb Q}_p$ and use valuation bounds; no compactness of their union is assumed.

For an algebraic group $H$ over a number field $M$, write $[H]=H(M)\backslash H(\mathbb A_M)$.

Measures on compact source groups and archimedean integration tori have algebraic normalization, and maximal compact subgroups have volume one at almost every finite place. Passing between these measures and invariant norm formulas costs a fixed global scalar. All toric sums called *raw* below are sums without division by the varying ring-class order. This distinction is essential for the estimates at the moving primes.

<a id="sec:tame"></a>
### A tame deformation and its Selmer length

We retain the fields and the contradiction assumption of <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">2</a>. In particular, the conductor-one Heegner trace $y\in A_0(K)$ is torsion. Put $T=T_pA_0$ and $V=T\otimes_{\mathbb Z_p}\mathbb Q_p$. Initially all cohomology in this section has $\mathbb Q_p$ coefficients; extension to the fixed finite coefficient field $k$ is made by tensor product. Enlarge the finite, conjugation-stable set $S$ to contain the places over $p$, the bad places of $A_0$, and all the fixed conductors used below. It may be enlarged by a further *fixed* finite set at any stage. In particular, the auxiliary finite characters and tests of Section <a href="#sec:content" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:content">6</a> are chosen independently of the moving primes, and their conductors are included before the final prime sequence is realized. All the constructions below allow this fixed enlargement. Write $G_{K,S}$ for the Galois group unramified outside $S$.

We need two conclusions for the same tame deformation. The one-sided local condition, full at one $p$-adic place and zero at its conjugate, will give a cohomology module of length zero or at most two. Under the torsion assumption on $y$, the first one or three coefficients of the corresponding weighted Heegner logarithms will tend to zero, respectively. These are [prop:green-length,prop:heegner-log] [prop:green-length](#prop:green-length), [prop:heegner-log](#prop:heegner-log). The logarithm estimate supplies the constant-term estimate of <a href="#prop:constant-term-small" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:constant-term-small">21</a>; the length bound will contradict the extensions extracted in Section <a href="#sec:extensions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:extensions">9</a>.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### The undeformed Selmer spaces at the two places above $p$

<a id="eq:away-p-vanishing"></a>
The two places $v,v^c$ of $K$ above $p$ have completion $\mathbb Q_p$. The alternating Weil pairing $$e:V\times V\longrightarrow\mathbb Q_p(1)$$ and the local invariant give a symmetric perfect pairing on $H^1(K_w,V)$: the sign from interchanging two degree-one cochains cancels the sign of $e$. The local Kummer line $H^1_f(K_w,V)$ is its own orthogonal complement. Thus, for $w\mid p$, this is a hyperbolic plane. These statements include arbitrary reduction at $p$: local torsion is finite, the rational Kummer space has dimension one, and the local Euler characteristic gives $\dim H^1(K_w,V)=2$. At a finite place $w\nmid p$ we instead have $$\label{eq:away-p-vanishing}
 H^0(K_w,V)=H^2(K_w,V)=H^1(K_w,V)=0.$$ Indeed $H^0=0$ by finiteness of local torsion, $H^2=0$ by local duality, and the Euler characteristic away from $p$ is zero. We use the local and global duality sequences in (Milne 2006, chap. I, Sections 2–4) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Milne1986](#ref-Milne1986)] and (Neukirch, Schmidt, and Wingberg 2008, vol. 323, sec. 8.6) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[NSW2008](#ref-NSW2008)].

For the moment let $W$ denote either $V$ or its twist $V^{DD'}$ over $K$. Its compact Selmer space has dimension one by <a href="#prop:arithmetic-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:arithmetic-reduction">5</a>. Let $H^1_{\mathrm{rel}}(K,W)$ have full conditions at $v,v^c$, and the usual unramified conditions outside $S$; conditions at the other places of $S$ make no difference by <a href="#eq:away-p-vanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:away-p-vanishing">[eq:away-p-vanishing]</a>. Let $H^1_{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}(K,W)$ have full condition at $v$ and zero condition at $v^c$, and let $H^1_{\overline{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}}(K,W)$ have the opposite conditions. These are the two one-sided Greenberg conditions.

<a id="stmt-lem-base-green"></a>
<div id="lem:base-green" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:base-green-bases"></a>
**Lemma 6**. *If the compact Selmer line of $W/K$ has nonzero localization, then $H^1_{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}(K,W)=H^1_{\overline{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}}(K,W)=0$. The only remaining possibility is the following *strict case* in the $V$ summand: $r=1$, and the compact Selmer space is generated by a class $a$ such that $a^c=a$ and every localization of $a$ is zero. In that case $$\label{eq:base-green-bases}
 H^1_{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}(K,V)=\langle a,h\rangle,
 \qquad
 H^1_{\overline{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}}(K,V)=\langle a,h^c\rangle,$$ where $h$ has nonzero localization at $v$ and zero localization at $v^c$. The $V^{DD'}$ summand always has zero Greenberg space.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-base-green"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-base-green</summary>

*Proof.* Let $F_w=H^1_f(K_w,W)$ and let $Q_w=H^1(K_w,W)/F_w$. Poitou–Tate duality identifies the image of $$H^1_{\mathrm{rel}}(K,W)\longrightarrow Q_v\oplus Q_{v^c}$$ with the annihilator of the localization of the compact Selmer line in $F_v\oplus F_{v^c}$. If that localization is nonzero, both of its coordinates are nonzero: the one-dimensional global space is stable under $c$, which interchanges the two places. Its annihilator is therefore a line whose two coordinates are both nonzero for every nonzero vector. A class satisfying a zero condition at one place has zero singular image, hence belongs to the compact Selmer line; the zero condition then kills that line as well.

Suppose the compact Selmer line is everywhere locally zero. The same duality sequence makes the singular image all of $Q_v\oplus Q_{v^c}$. The kernel of localization is precisely the compact Selmer line. Consequently the relaxed local image has dimension two and maps isomorphically to the singular plane. In each local hyperbolic plane choose the isotropic complement $E_w$ of $F_w$, choosing the two complements compatibly with $c$. The relaxed local image is the graph of a map $$E_v\oplus E_{v^c}\longrightarrow F_v\oplus F_{v^c}.$$ Global reciprocity makes the graph isotropic. In mutually dual bases its matrix is therefore skew-symmetric, hence of the form $\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&u\\-u&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$. Conjugation interchanges both pairs of basis vectors and preserves the local pairings. Invariance of the graph therefore changes $u$ to $-u$, so $u=0$. Lifting the first coordinate line gives $h$; conjugating gives $h^c$ and proves <a href="#eq:base-green-bases" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:base-green-bases">[eq:base-green-bases]</a>.

The auxiliary summand has a nontorsion global point and nonzero localization, by <a href="#prop:arithmetic-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:arithmetic-reduction">5</a>. If $r=0$, the unique Selmer line of $V/K$ likewise comes from a nontorsion point on the quadratic twist $A_0^D/\mathbb Q$, so it cannot be strict. When $r=1$, the twist by $D$ has zero Selmer space and the line comes from $H^1_f(\mathbb Q,V)$, giving $a^c=a$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### Primes with a controlled tame logarithm

In the prime construction and cohomological calculations of this section, Galois Frobenius elements are arithmetic Frobenius elements. The following construction gives a tame direction that is trivial at the fixed places and has uniform local bounds. In the strict case we will refine its limiting Frobenius element after determining the obstruction to first-order lifting.

<a id="stmt-prop-tame-primes"></a>
<div id="prop:tame-primes" class="proposition">

**Proposition 7**. *There are rational primes $q_i\notin S$, split completely in $L$, distinguished places $\mathfrak q_i$ of $K$ above them, cyclic $p$-quotients $\Delta_i$ of the ring class group of conductor $q_i$, and surjections $$\ell_i:G_K\longrightarrow\Delta_i\simeq\mathbb Z/p^{n_i}\mathbb Z,
 \qquad n_i\longrightarrow\infty,$$ with the following properties.*

1.  *$\ell_i$ is unramified outside the pair above $q_i$, its restriction to inertia at $\mathfrak q_i$ is surjective, $\ell_i^c=-\ell_i$, and $\ell_i|_{G_{K_s}}=0$ for every $s\in S$.*

2.  *$v_p(q_i-1)-n_i$ is bounded. At either place above $p$, the full ring class field of conductor $q_i$ is unramified and its local degree has bounded $p$-part.*

3.  *Frobenius at $\mathfrak q_i$, on all fixed data unramified outside $S$, tends to an element $\gamma\in G_L$ such that $$\epsilon(\gamma)=1,\qquad
     \det(V(\gamma)-1)\ne0,\qquad
     \mathcal C(\gamma)\text{ is not a root of unity}.$$ Moreover $\gamma$ fixes all $p$-power radicals of a fixed, conjugation-stable finite list in $K^\times$ spanning $\mathcal O_{K,S}^{\times}\otimes\mathbb Q_p$ under the Kummer map.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-tame-primes"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-tame-primes</summary>

*Proof.* Choose representatives $\mathfrak a_j$ for generators of the ideal class group, prime to $S$. For their orders, their multiplication relations, and the expressions of the primes in $S$ in these generators, choose principal generators. Adjoin generators of the finite-rank $S$-unit group and their conjugates to this finite list, denoted $\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_d$. Fixed class-group orders and roots of unity will cost only a fixed index.

For a split prime $q$, the kernel of the ring class group map to the ordinary ideal class group is the quotient of $(\mathcal O_K/q\mathcal O_K)^\times$ by diagonal residue units and global units. Its $p$-part is cyclic, of order $p^{v_p(q-1)}$ up to a fixed factor. Require the $\alpha_j$ to have zero residue logarithm modulo $p^{n_i}$ at the pair over $q_i$. This is ensured by splitting in their $p^{n_i}$-radical extensions, together with the corresponding roots of unity. The ring class presentation now allows us to set the logarithms of all $\mathfrak a_j$ equal to zero and to take the surjective logarithm on the residue quotient. Each relation is respected, and the expressions chosen for the primes of $S$ give $\ell_i|_{G_{K_s}}=0$. Conjugation inverts the residue quotient and the ring class action, giving $\ell_i^c=-\ell_i$.

We verify that imposing the full radical condition leaves room for $\gamma$. Over the cyclotomic tower the Kummer Galois group is a closed subgroup of $\mathbb Z_p(1)^d$. The joint image of $V$ and $\mathcal C$ is a $p$-adic Lie group. A common Lie quotient with this translation group is abelian, so it kills the derived Lie algebra of the image of $V$. On the remaining torus Lie algebra, conjugation by an open cyclotomic subgroup is trivial; on the Kummer translations it is multiplication by the cyclotomic character. Any equivariant common quotient is consequently zero. This argument can be made after a fixed finite extension, which does not change Lie dimensions. Thus killing the radicals removes no Lie dimension from the joint image over the cyclotomic tower.

For a non-CM elliptic curve the determinant-one image has Lie algebra $\mathfrak{sl}_2$, by the open image theorem (Serre 1972) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Serre1972](#ref-Serre1972)]; for a CM curve its identity component contains the norm-one torus, by the CM description of the Tate-module character (Serre 1968) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[Serre1968](#ref-Serre1968)]. In either case the locus $\det(V(g)-1)\ne0$ is nonempty and open in every sufficiently small identity neighborhood. The infinity type of $\mathcal C$ makes its restriction over the cyclotomic tower nonconstant; its torsion locus is a proper closed subset in a sufficiently small identity neighborhood. The two complements intersect. We may also impose $g\in G_L$ and any fixed open-kernel conditions. This gives $\gamma$ fixing the full radicals and the cyclotomic tower with the required properties.

It remains to control $v_p(q_i-1)$, rather than merely make it large. The cyclotomic–radical image is a closed subgroup of $$\mathbb Z_p(1)^d\rtimes\mathbb Z_p^\times$$ with open cyclotomic projection. Choose an element in a pro-$p$ subgroup whose cyclotomic projection is nontorsion. Its $p^m$-th powers tend to the identity; their translation coordinates are divisible by $p^{m-C_0}$, and the valuation of their cyclotomic coordinate minus one is $m+C_1$, for fixed $C_0,C_1$. Multiplying $\gamma$ by such a power with $m=n_i+C_2$ therefore leaves all radicals trivial modulo the requested precision and makes the cyclotomic valuation exactly $n_i+C$, for a fixed $C$. Chebotarev applied to a growing sequence of finite quotients realizes these conditions and increasingly accurate approximations on all fixed data. Choosing a place above each prime selects the indicated Frobenius representative. The primes can be taken outside any fixed finite set.

Finally the $p$-part of the order of the full ring class group is $p^{v_p(q_i-1)+O(1)}$. The kernel of its map to $\Delta_i$ therefore has bounded $p$-part. The Frobenius of every $s\in S$ belongs to this kernel. At $p$ the extension is unramified, so the $p$-part of the local degree, which is the $p$-part of the Frobenius order, is bounded. This proves the proposition. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### Admissible cochains and division by the parameter

<a id="eq:binomial-loss"></a>
Fix a nonprincipal ultrafilter $\mathcal U$ on the prime indices. A statement “eventually” in the construction below means on a set in $\mathcal U$. For every fixed $b\ge1$, put $$R_b=k[t]/(t^b).$$ At finite stages the substitution of $(1+t)^{\ell_i}$ is defined only to finite $p$-adic precision. There is a constant $c_b$ such that $$\label{eq:binomial-loss}
 u\equiv u'\pmod {p^n}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \binom{u}{j}\equiv\binom{u'}{j}\pmod {p^{n-c_b}}
 \quad(0\le j<b).$$ This follows by writing each binomial coefficient as a polynomial with denominator $j!$. Thus truncated substitution of $(1+t)^{\ell_i}$ defines an action modulo $p^{n_i-c_b}$ and sends integral group-ring elements of $\Delta_i$ to coefficients with a fixed denominator. No division by $|\Delta_i|$ is involved.

For a sequence $(g_i)$, let $\ell((g_i))$ be the $p$-adic ultralimit of $\ell_i(g_i)\in\mathbb Z/p^{n_i}\mathbb Z$: each fixed residue modulo $p^m$ is well-defined eventually and has a unique ultralimit. This is an additive character on the group of sequences of elements of $G_{K,Sq_i}$, modulo equality on a set in $\mathcal U$. We can therefore define $$\psi=(1+t)^\ell,\qquad
 M_b^{\eta}=V\otimes_{\mathbb Q_p}R_b(\psi^{\eta}),
 \qquad \eta\in\{1,-1\}.$$ Constant representations have their usual compact $p$-adic ultralimit.

<a id="stmt-def-admissible"></a>
<div id="def:admissible" class="definition">

**Definition 8**. An *admissible cochain* with coefficients in $M_b^{\eta}$ is a function on sequences of Galois elements which is the coefficientwise ultralimit of continuous finite-stage cochains, in the fixed basis of $V\otimes R_b$, with one denominator $p^C$ independent of $i$. Finite-stage cochain identities are required modulo precisions $A_i\longrightarrow\infty$ along $\mathcal U$, with $A_i\le n_i-c_b$.
